Khawaja 'hurt' by 'personal attacks' from Cummins in sandpaper denials

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Usman Khawaja hopes there is collective responsibility for the Sandpapergate scandal in time and says current captain Pat Cummins still has the "opportunity of power"; former batter says Cameron Bancroft feels like a "different person" after finally telling his side of ball-tampering saga

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Usman Khawaja has told the Sky Sports Cricket Podcast that he was "hurt" by Pat Cummins' "personal attacks" on him as the Australia Test captain explosively disputed his claims that every member of the side was aware of the use of sandpaper in 2018.

Khawaja made the allegations in his autobiography Sabr and they have since been backed up by David Warner and Cameron Bancroft - two of the players, along with then-skipper Steve Smith, who were slapped with bans following the ball-tampering plot in South Africa.

Cummins, part of the Australia bowling attack on that tour eight years ago, insisted he was not privy to the scheme when he and fellow quicks Josh Hazlewood and Mitchell Starc, as well as spinner Nathan Lyon, released a joint-statement in 2021.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

He then categorially denied knowledge earlier this month in a 68-minute press conference in which he accused Khawaja of being "casual" with people's lives.

Khawaja said in a chat with Michael Atherton and Nasser Hussain: "It was the most surprised I've been for a long time. I wasn't expecting that [from Cummins] whatsoever.

"I thought Pat had this really great opportunity to come out and bring everyone together. He's such a great leader. I've said in the book that I love the person he is, love the leader he is, so I thought if anyone can bring us together, it's Pat.

"I had hoped that he would - I text him before, trying do anything I could to make it easier, to do something to bring everyone in - but I realised that wasn't going to happen when he first started talking, so that was really sad.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"Then obviously the personal attacks were sad. I'm not going to lie about that, they hurt from a friend, someone you really respect. That wasn't very Pat Cummins-like, very different to the Pat Cummins I know, and it's hard for me to judge him on that.

"I know Pat, I know his family. They're beautiful, so it was really hard to reconcile the Pat I knew and the Pat that was attacking me personally rather than attacking what I'm saying. That was by far one of the toughest days I've ever experienced.

"I'll caveat that by saying that I have nothing but love for Pat. I don't judge people on certain instances or whatnot. That's not how I live my life. That's not how I function.

"Right now, are we talking? No, not really, but I've still got a lot of hope in my heart that at some stage if there's anyone that can bring everyone together, it's Pat. He's got the opportunity of power, whether it's now or later.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"But I think there was a perfect opportunity there if he took a softer route. He took a real harsh route and I think because of that everything just blew up after that like even more."

Khawaja wanted the team to take collective responsibility for the sandpaper plot at the time but says he was talked out of it by Smith as only the skipper, Warner and Bancroft - the player caught on camera looking to manipulate the ball - were suspended.

The 39-year-old, who retired from international cricket following Australia's 4-1 Ashes thrashing of England last winter, is hopeful "accountability" will eventually come.

He said: "For me it was about the team, Cricket Australia, coming together and saying, 'look, we didn't live up to our standards, we didn't live up to our values and three guys suffered for that'.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"I think there is a way to collectively take this still.

"Will everyone like agree on this? I can't see it happening now the way it's gotten, but for me the middle ground is the collective responsibility and accountability as a team.

"We win together, we lose together, we get caught for ball tampering, we take it together. I think that's achievable, but I can't speak for everyone."

Khawaja explained that he became extremely close to Bancroft on the 2019 Ashes tour of England when the latter returned to the side for the first time since 'Sandpapergate' and played what, as things currently stand, are his final two Test matches.

Bancroft's detail-filled, 4,000-word-plus account of the sandpaper deception dropped in the West Australian newspaper on the day of Australia's third ODI against in Hobart, adding even more intrigue to a story that has rocked, and gripped, cricket.

Khawaja said of his relationship with Bancroft: "In the 2019 Ashes, we both played, we got both got dropped and we would play Uno pretty much every day. If I kind of knew him at the start, I knew him really well by the end because we talked about everything.

"I remember asking him about the whole sandpaper incident once, too, He was still working his way through it at the time, trying to figure out how he's dealing with this.

"In his mind I assume he was thinking, 'I'll play 50 more games, become a legend and then I'll earn the respect back of the people'. Unfortunately it didn't work out that way for him and I think that's the thing that's really played on his mind throughout this time.

"I've kept in contact with him periodically. I remember being sat on the bus with him maybe four years ago and I brought this topic up again, asking why he didn't come out and talk about this. He said 'I can't'. He said it to me a few weeks ago actually."

Khawaja continued of Bancroft: "He got in touch with me because he was really struggling. I talked to him on the phone for an hour and a half and told him what I was planning to write in my book.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"That was about halfway during that conversation. The second half of that conversation, I could feel his energy change, because the weight and burden of trying to carry this was eating him up. In his mind, he was thinking, 'no, I'm not the only one here'.

"The first day it came out, he sent me a text saying, 'Uzzy, all I want to say is you've done the right thing and I appreciate this more than you can ever imagine'. That that was one of the things that kept me going because everyone was nailing me that first day.

"We do have a good relationship and a good rapport. He is one of the guys throughout the last two weeks who has been grateful and I needed that, I'm going to be honest, because at times I was like, 'have I done the right thing? Why am I doing this?'

"He finally got to put out a statement. I talked to him afterwards and he's chill, he's laughing, he's joking like he hasn't joked with me in eight years. I can tell that he's a different person.

"If that's the silver lining, it makes it worth it. I can never quite fathom what he went through in those eight years."

2026-27 NBA Rookies Report Card 2.0, Grades Nov. 25: Two players get an A: Cameron Boozer, Yaxel Landeborg

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

No one is going to confuse me with a Harvard professor, but it feels like "grade inflation" has hit Rookie Report Cards 2.0. Turns out, this highly touted draft class is largely living up to its billing, with many of these players not just showing promise (if not efficiency yet), but also contributing to winning. There are a lot of good grades, and anything below a B feels a bit harsh this early in the season (and their careers). With that intro, here's our second round of Rookie Report Cards.

2026-27 Stats: 16.6 points, 9.2 rebounds, 5.3 assists, 41.7% shooting

What Boozer is doing with Memphis is what he has done at every stop on his basketball journey: The stats are impressive, but they don't begin to tell the whole story. Boozer simply contributes to winning, doing whatever the team needs on a given night, and he has been completely at ease as the face of the franchise since he walked in the door. He has been everything the Grizzlies hoped for when they drafted him.

Boozer has been a big part of Memphis' fast start, as has their depth — and that depth will be put to the test for the next month with Boozer out due to a hamstring strain. Injuries suck.

2026-27 Stats: 19.2 points, 7.4 rebounds, 3.6 assists, 45.2% shooting

Heading into last June's draft, the league-wide consensus was that the top four picks were in a class of their own, and everyone else was a tier below. Lendeborg has blown that logic up early this season and should be considered a frontrunner for Rookie of the Year. He ranks near the top of this rookie class in pretty much every statistical category — points (second), rebounds (second), blocks (fourth) — and his lowest is sixth in assists. He's shooting 36.7% from 3-point range and has been incredibly efficient for a rookie.

Things have been tougher for Lendeborg since Stephen Curry went out and he became the guy at the top of opponent scouting reports — asking him to lead the offense and be the focus of the defense is a lot for a rookie. That said, Lendeborg has still played well and is exactly the guy the Warriors needed.

2026-27 Stats: 7.9 points, 2.7 rebounds, 4.2 assists, 48.8% shooting

It took just nine games for Flemmings to replace veteran CJ McCollum in the starting lineup in Atlanta, a sign of how good he's looked to start the season — and how good he's looked in practices to coach Quin Snyder and the staff. He has just returned from missing a few games with a back issue.

He is a two-way player who brings physicality and defends well. In Summer League, his decision-making off the pick-and-roll showed promise — and he has carried that over to the regular season, especially since moving into the starting lineup. What was unimpressive at Summer League was his shooting, but he has found his form to start the season, and while the volume is low, he is knocking down 40% of his 3s so far this season. Atlanta may just have the point guard they were seeking.

2026-27 Stats: 11.4 points, 5.7 rebounds, 2.5 assists, 45.5% shooting

I am higher on Burries play so far than many others, and this early-season grade might be optimistic, but I like what I see. Maybe nobody is noticing because few people are watching Milwaukee right now, but once Taylor Jenkins moved Burries back to the bench a couple of games ago, it seemed to unlock something, he's playing much more free and much more efficiently. On the season, he has a below-average 52.4 true shooting percentage as a starter, but that jumps to an impressive 63 as a reserve.

There is legitimate concern about his 3-point shot (now up to 31.3% for the season), but just watching him play, Burries is a reason for hope for a Bucks fanbase that needs something to get behind at the start of a rebuilding process.

2026-27 Stats: 12.7 points, 2.7 rebounds, 1.8 assists, 47.2% shooting

Acuff's running mate at Arkansas, who fell to the No. 34 pick, looks like the steal of the second round. This grade is based partly on the expectations vs. production the Cavaliers have gotten from him — he's started three games and is getting 18+ minutes a night, something impressive for any rookie.

Thomas is a shooter, knocking down 40.7% on 3-pointers. We should have seen this coming when he led Summer League in scoring, averaging 27.3 points per game. He's got room to grow — he isn't much of a playmaker yet and isn't driving and drawing a lot of fouls — but he is knocking down shots in a league where that is a highly prized skill.

2026-27 Stats: 16.5 points, 6.9 rebounds, 2.8 assists, 42.2% shooting

It's a little hard to grade Dybantsa because it feels like he's being limited by his environment. This man is a strong, physical driver who has to wait his turn as the third guy in the pecking order behind Anthony Davis (great start to the season) and Trae Young (um... not as great a start to the season, let's say). Plus, Alex Sarr is a quality young big, but he's not spacing the floor or creating driving lanes. Still, we see Dybantsa's undeniable talent shine through at moments.

Dybantsa seems to be breaking through of late, with three double-doubles in his last four games and 19.8 points and 10.3 rebounds in those contests. The Wizards need to build on that and feature Dybantsa more and let him grow and learn through some mistakes. His grade is this low only because a left ankle sprain delayed his start to the season and, with that, his development is maybe a little behind where it could be, but he's starting to really demand more time on the ball and more opportunities.

2026-27 Stats: 15.9 points, 4.5 rebounds, 4 assists, 40.1% shooting

Kind of like Dybantsa in Washington, the challenge with Peterson in Utah has been fit. You can try to label Peterson and Utah's other young guard, Keyonte George, as point and shooting guards (in whatever order you wish), the reality is they are both scoring guards who want to attack. Then, take Lauri Markkanen and Jaren Jackson Jr. out of the picture in Utah due to injuries and it's just hard to get a read on Peterson at times.

That said, Peterson is improving — he had a stretch of five 20+ point games a few weeks ago — and more importantly, he's getting to the rim more and not settling for midrange jumpers as he did earlier in the season. He is at his best when he is an aggressive, downhill attacker reading the defense, and the Jazz need to encourage more of that and less of him pulling up or taking floaters. Let him be himself, working through the growing pains.

2026-27 Stats: 24.6 points, 3.4 rebounds, 7.2 assists, 40.3% shooting

While some high draft picks are having to stand in line to get touches and opportunities, that is not the case with Acuff — he is at the front of the line for the all-you-can-eat buffet of shots in Sacramento. He's third in the league in shot attempts per game. He actually leads the NBA in average time of possession — he has the ball for 7.2 minutes per game.

The Kings' record this season is what you get when you turn the keys to the offense over to a 6'2" rookie guard, and no doubt his efficiency and ability to lift up teammates must get better. What you have to like is his relentlessness, and he shows he knows how to read the pick-and-roll. There is a lot to build on, but Acuff also has a long way to go. As do the Kings.

2026-27 Stats: 10 points, 7.1 rebounds, 1.9 assists, 64.9% shooting

Thrust into a larger role with Chet Holmgren out, what you have to like about Mara is that he plays within himself — he knows what he does well, where he can impact games, and he stays in that box. He's not going to space the floor (which is why the double-big lineups with him and Isaiah Hartenstein can have issues). What he can do is protect the paint, set screens and score around the rim. His defense has been fantastic, and the Thunder have needed that as they battle through a rash of injuries.

Caleb Wilson, Grade: Incomplete (B- in games he played)

2026-27 Stats: 18.9 points, 6.2 rebounds, 1.9 assists, 42% shooting

He's only played in 10 games due to a fractured hand, which is a setback in his development and the reason for the incomplete grade (something that will change later in the season). It's also a freak injury beyond his control, a fluke.

If we base the grade on the 10 games he played, you see a player with immense talent — maybe the highest ceiling of any player in this class — who has already put together an impressive highlight reel.nBut he is so raw and making so many rookie mistakes — turnovers, missed 3s that he should pass on — it's hard to grade him. Do we grade him on the potential? Then it's an "A," no question. However, if we're judging him as a rookie player, as I have done, the grade drops, maybe B- is too harsh, but it's where I landed. For now. I expect that by the end of the season his grade will be much higher. He will figure it out.

2026-27: 11.2 points, 3.5 rebounds, 2.9 assists, 34.4% shooting

Part of the challenge in grading Wagler is that he comes off the bench at a stacked position for the team. Wagler is in the same space as veterans Derrick Jones Jr., Kris Dunn and Max Strus, and Tyronn Lue needs to showcase those veterans for potential trades. Lue has admitted he needs to find Wagler more minutes.

There are a lot of things Wagler does well, he can knock down 3s (he's been impressive as a shooter of late after a slow start), and he knows how to get to the free throw line. The concern is the 34% shooting on two-pointers, a lot of them from floater range. All of that should improve, and his game will come around if he gets enough minutes on the court.

2026-27: 6.3 points, 4.7 rebounds, 1.4 assists, 42.2% shooting

This grade may be a little harsh for Johnson, who had injuries slowing him in training camp and is just 20 years old (Johnson is more on Cooper Flagg's timeline than the 24-year-old Lendeborg). It's far, far too early to write off Johnson. My questions are: Did the Mavericks draft the third-best player on coach Dusty May's championship Michigan team, and would Mara have been a better fit in Dallas?

That said, he has struggled with fouls on the defensive end, and is not converting enough of his offensive opportunities. There are flashes, but he has a long way to go to be the long-term forward pairing with Cooper Flagg that the Mavericks drafted him to be. It's early in the season and his career, but Johnson has a lot of work to do.

Others receiving consideration: Mikel Brown Jr., Brooklyn (injuries and a minutes limit have held him back so far, but he shows real promise); Trevon Brazile, Denver (the best defensive rookie in this class, already a plus defender); Christian Anderson, Charlotte; Allen Graves, Toronto; Bennett Stirtz, Oklahoma City.

Next Lincoln City manager: Chris Cohen, Tom Shaw sacked by Championship club

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Darren Plant | 25 Nov 2026 12:59, Last updated: 25 Nov 2026 12:59

Lincoln City have sacked co-managers Chris Cohen and Tom Shaw.

The pair were promoted from the club's backroom staff after head coach Michael Skubala made the decision to leave the League One champions for Bristol City in the summer.

With Lincoln sitting in 21st place in the Championship table and two points above the relegation zone, a positive spin can be put on the club's first campaign in the second tier of English football for more than 60 years.

However, Tuesday's 4-0 defeat at Charlton Athletic was Lincoln's fifth successive loss in the Championship.

As well as one win and eight defeats being posted across a 10-match period, the club's hierarchy have decided for a change in direction.

A statement read: "We have worked incredibly hard to reach the Championship and will do everything in our power to retain our status in this division.”   "We know how much this football club means to our supporters and are humbled by the level of support we’ve had home and away so far this season."

ℹ️ City can confirm Chris Cohen and Tom Shaw have been relieved of their duties as head coaches with immediate effect. We thank them for their contributions to the club over the last several years and wish them well for the future. https://t.co/mwLolWdxhp

Lincoln add that they will next provide an update on their search for a successor to Cohen and Shaw on November 30.

Despite 15 points coming from 17 matches, Cohen and Shaw can have no complaints over Lincoln's decision.

With Preston North End and Burnley - in 22nd and 23rd position - closing the gap, Lincoln's owners are justified in the move that they have taken.

Furthermore, second-placed Wolverhampton Wanderers are the next visitors to the ProAmpac Stadium on Saturday afternoon.

Likely viewing that game as a free-hit under first-team coach Stuart Pearson, Lincoln will look to have a permanent manager in place for the trip to Cardiff City on December 5.

Raheem Sterling avoids jail for £270,000 Lamborghini crash: England star handed suspended prison sentence after telling court he underwent 12-day rehab stint for laughing gas 'problem'

📅 2026-11-25  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 04:50 EST, 25 November 2026 | Updated: 08:13 EST, 25 November 2026

Raheem Sterling has been given a suspended prison sentence for dangerous driving and possession of nitrous oxide - avoiding prison time after it was revealed he spent 12 days in rehab.

The 31-year-old former England footballer was sentenced to 12 months in jail, suspended for 18, at Basingstoke Magistrates' Court on Wednesday. Alongside the suspended sentence, he must complete 100 hours of community service and has been banned from driving for two years. He was also ordered to pay a victim surcharge of £187 and prosecution costs of £85.

In her sentencing remarks, District Judge Kirsty Allman revealed that Sterling consulted UK addiction treatment on June 15 and went into rehab for 12 days.

She explained that nitrous oxide had 'become a maladaptive coping mechanism during a difficult period in his career', adding that he was a 'role model' before passing the sentence that saw Sterling avoid serving prison time.

While his sentence does not mandate therapeutic intervention, the lead therapist at Banbury Lodge - where Sterling checked in - feels further treatment would be ‘beneficial’.

His MBE, charitable work and time spent in rehabilitation were cited as mitigating factors, helping him avoid a prison sentence.

Raheem Sterling is pictured leaving Basingstoke Magistrates' Court on Wednesday, shaking hands with his solicitor Leo Martin - he avoided prison time

Sterling is seen after being pulled over by police earlier this year while under the influence of laughing gas in his Lamborghini - he later spent 12 days in rehab

Allman added: 'You are 31 years old and a man of previous good character. I accept your character has been positively exemplary.

‘(But) these offences are so serious that neither a fine nor a community order can be justified. The custodial threshold is clearly crossed, and custody is unavoidable.’

That said, custody was avoided because the law was changed in March 2026, with custodial sentences of one year or less presumed to be suspended - unless there are specific aggravating features - because of the overcrowded prison population.

Sterling - who wore trainers, a black quarter-zip jumper with a white t-shirt and black trousers - froze as he waited for the judge to enter the courtroom on Wednesday. He sat with his back against the wall in the glass dock and kept eye contact with her while she read her sentencing remarks.

The former Manchester City and Chelsea attacker pleaded guilty after crashing his £270,000 Lamborghini in May while driving under the influence of laughing gas.

Four police forces received reports about his dangerous driving before he eventually wrecked his car into a metal barrier on the A327 during rush hour on the morning of May 28.

During the arrest, Sterling told police he had 'a problem' with laughing gas after subjecting rush hour motorists to a terrifying 90 minutes of dangerous driving, in which he zig-zagged on the M3 and M25 motorways before swerving onto a slip road and crashing into a metal gate. Police found six canisters of nitrous oxide in his car.

In the police station, Sterling was asked to judge 30 seconds with his eyes closed but was so impaired that he recorded 52.

Temporary chief inspector Chris Sharland of Hampshire Police said: ‘Nitrous oxide, commonly known as laughing gas or NOS, is a Class C drug, and driving whilst under its influence is an emerging threat to the safety of motorists on our roads.

Sterling leaves the court with his solicitor having been given a suspended sentence

Image issued by the Crown Prosecution Service shows the damage to the Lamborghini Urus being driven by Sterling

‘Driving whilst impaired with nitrous oxide can cause unconsciousness, and in the worst-case scenarios neurological damage or death, so there is a very real risk of causing serious harm to yourself or other motorists.

‘We urge drivers to be aware of not only the law, but the risk this type of behaviour carries – one decision can have devastating consequences.’

In his pre-sentencing remarks, Sterling's lawyer, Jason Bartfeld KC, said Sterling is 'more concerned not by damage to his reputation' but the 'consequences' it has had for his family, particularly his children.

He added: 'Mr Sterling recognised the various unhealthy coping mechanisms that brought him to this position and has sought to address these in the most positive and effective way and with some success.

‘All of it makes it extremely sad and a matter of his own personal regret, tarnishing his own personal reputation.

'The end point of this is that he is absolutely determined that he will never appear before a criminal court again.'

In May, police bodycam footage showed the moment that Sterling was arrested with canisters and a deflated balloon on his lap.

An officer could be heard saying 'you can put that down' to the footballer after opening his car door.

He could then be seen taking the items from his lap and putting them behind his seat.

Sterling gets back in the car after his sentencing and is banned from driving for two years

The former Manchester City and Chelsea star, who won the PFA Young Player of the Year award in 2018-19, was then read his rights as he is arrested after being asked to put his arms behind his back.

His arrest, near the Minley interchange on the M3 in Hampshire, happened four weeks after he had returned to the UK following the end of an abortive attempt to revive his football career at Dutch club Feyenoord.

Sterling, from Berkshire, pleaded guilty in September to dangerous driving, possession of nitrous oxide for wrongful inhalation and failing to provide a specimen after a single vehicle collision at 8.45am on May 28. Police found six cannisters of nitrous oxide in his car.

During that hearing, Basingstoke Magistrates' Court heard that Sterling was so impaired by the laughing gas that he was unable to perform basic police station tests like placing his finger on his nose or placing one foot in front of another.

Barrister Simon Jones, prosecuting, said of Sterling: 'He appeared to be quiet, withdrawn and lethargic and actively fidgeting.

'Eye lids drooping consistent with fatigue or drug impairment. He said he was feeling very cold, despite this being in the middle of a heatwave with 33-degree temperatures.'

The 31-year-old was charged with dangerous driving and possession of laughing gas after swerving his car on the M25 and M3

Motorist Fiona Jennings told police she had looked across to witness the driver of the Lamborghini with a balloon in front of his face which was so big she thought it was an air bag.

It was so big that 'she had no idea how the driver would have been able to see the road ahead,' Mr Jones said. Ms Jennings described Sterling's driving as 'absolutely horrendous' and expressed 'genuine concern that the driver may kill someone.'

Evidence previously presented to court suggested Sterling was also inhaling laughing gas while at the wheel of the Lamborghini a full seven hours before his arrest.

A concerned witness called police after seeing the driver of the same car entering a McDonalds car park alarmingly fast at 1.10am the same morning and seeing the driver holding up 'a pink, inflated balloon.'

By 7.45am that morning, another motorist concerned by the Lamborghini erratically changing lanes, saw the driver placing a white balloon to his mouth and inhaling its contents.

For 30 minutes, that motorist watched the vehicle weave from inside to outside lane and back and come close to smashing into the central reservation.

Sterling will now attempt to put the shameful episode behind him and move on with his life and career having avoided prison time.

Erling Haaland sparks fear among fans after concerning TV interview as he admits he is 'questioning everything' after Man City's draw with Napoli

📅 2026-11-25  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 04:06 EST, 25 November 2026 | Updated: 08:33 EST, 25 November 2026

Manchester City fans have expressed concern over Erling Haaland's despondent interview following their Champions League draw with Napoli, with the star admitting he is 'questioning everything' amid their slump in form.

Enzo Maresca's side rescued a late draw at the Etihad on Tuesday night, with Rayan Cherki equalising in the 86th minute to secure a 2-2 draw with Napoli.

The result, however, continued an underwhelming run of form with Man City having won just twice in their last seven matches – one of which was achieved in a penalty shoot-out against Brighton in the Carabao Cup.

Man City have suffered defeats to Aston Villa, Brighton and Nottingham Forest in the Premier League in the run, as well as drawing with Fulham on the weekend.

Haaland, who captained Man City against Napoli, admitted post-match to TNT Sports that their underwhelming form has left the squad questioning themselves.

'We were not able to control and attack the game in the way we want,' Haaland said. 'Football is easy and difficult. When you win it's easy and you know what to do. When you don't you start questioning everything.

Erling Haaland cut a despondent figure as he admitted to 'questioning everything' after Man City's draw with Napoli continued their underwhelming form

🗣️ "I have no idea what we were missing. You could feel it in the last few games that there's something that's not clicking fully."@Erling reflects on @ManCity's 2-2 draw with Napoli.🎤 @AndyKerrtv #UCL | #ManCity pic.twitter.com/Cx2Hzieom3

'The perfect thing would be to not think but it's not so easy.

'We have so much quality so it's about getting the quality out in every player. We have to regroup, stay positive and bring energy on the pitch and try to do our best.'

Haaland admitted in another interview with Bein Sports that the run of form 'hurts', with the star having offered a resigned laugh when asked whether the draw against Napoli should be considered a 'good point'.

The 26-year-old was seen looking down throughout the minute and a half interview, while he also rubbed his face with his hand as he looked to find answers for Man City's recent struggles.

'How the game was we managed to come back two times, but we demand more,' Haaland said.

'We have to do better and find some ways to control the game more and perform in a better way.

'I have no idea what we were missing. You could feel it in the last few games that it is not clicking fully and we have to find out, stay positive and get the best out of every single day.

'With and without the armband I feel huge responsibility because all I want to do is perform for this club and win games for Man City and right now we are not doing that. It hurts.'

Fans quickly noted Haaland's downbeat responses to questions and despondent body language during the interview.

Man City fans have expressed concerns over Haaland's downbeat responses and body language in the interview

Fans claimed they have never seen Haaland 'looking this flat' and suggested the star 'looks distraught' amid their underwhelming run

'I've never seen Haaland looking this flat. Not even close to it,' one commented.

'At least he’s being honest with himself. Looks distraught,' a second said, with a third writing 'Extremely concerning.'

'Never seen Erling Haaland look like this before,' another added, while a Man City fan wrote 'You can really tell how confused Haaland is from his expression. They don’t have confidence in their own football.'

One went as far as claiming 'Haaland looks like he’s done with Manchester City…' while sharing footage of his reaction to one of Napoli's goals on the night.

Other fans questioned whether Haaland and his Man City team-mates' recent slump may be related to concerns over the potential punishment the club could face for breaching financial rules.

Man City were found guilty of the vast majority of charges brought against them by the Premier League for financial wrongdoing when the verdict was released in September. The club have since appealed.

An independent commission ruled Man City had disguised payments worth £900million in a nine-year period with ‘sham’ deals.

Man City were found to have artificially inflated sponsorship agreements and pumped money into the club from its Abu Dhabi owners via disguised payments in a bid to swerve Premier League spending rules.

Man City have continued to insist they had 'irrefutable evidence' proving their innocence, but if the club loses its appeal, the Premier League have a range of sanctions at their disposal – including relegation or expulsion.

Man City face Arsenal in a crucial match on Sunday as they look to make inroads in the Gunners six point lead at the top of the Premier League

Haaland had pushed back against suggestions the case had affected their form during the international break, while stressing he was committed long-term to the club.

The forward's first answer to Norwegian broadcaster TV2 had spanned two minutes and 14 seconds, as he reeled off the teams he had represented from 2009 to 2018, the years covered by the charges after he was asked about the club's situation.

'In 2009, I played for Bryne U9s. In 2010, the U10s. In 2011, the U11s. 2012, the U12s, 2013, the U13s. Shall we continue? We don't need the whole list. 2014, the U14s. 2015, the U15s and the national team,' Haaland said.

'Then the U16s, I think. In 2016, I moved up to Bryne's first team, second team and youth team. In 2017, I went to Molde. In 2018, I was with Molde. The first team, Molde 2 and a bit with the youth team.

'So this is a question I struggle to answer because it's a legal battle…

'It's a case between the Premier League and Manchester City that a panel will deal with. Am I supposed to answer that? I'm not really sure.'

He continued: 'It's a difficult question to answer when I don't really know that much about it.

'I assume the answers will come… I don't know when. I haven't read much about it.

'To be completely honest, I'm focused on trying to perform for Manchester City.

The Man City star, who signed a nine-and-a-half-year contract in late 2024, told Norwegian television earlier this month that his focus was on helping the team to turn their form around.

The draw against Napoli leaves Man City lying sixth in the Champions League table - which would be enough to see Maresca's side to qualify for the last-16.

Man City face a crucial clash on the weekend when they face reigning champions Arsenal in the Premier League, as they look to halve the six-point gap to the leaders at the top of the table.

Sony Will Support The Witcher 4's Marketing, Much Like GTA 6

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Witcher 4 dev CDPR has signed a marketing arrangement with Sony, which will likely see the latter promote the upcoming RPG similarly to GTA 6.

PlayStation pushed Rockstar’s latest release ridiculously hard, claiming it ‘Plays Best on PS5’.

While that may only actually apply to the PS5 Pro version, the Japanese giant could argue that its base hardware – which drops a few more frames than the Xbox Series X – still has the advantage of deeper DualSense integration et al.

Irrespective of slogan, CDPR’s CFO Piotr Nielubowicz told investors today that his company recently signed a new arrangement with Sony, which includes “the presence of our games on PlayStation and in PS Plus subscriptions, as well as our collaboration during the upcoming The Witcher 4 marketing campaign”.

Nielubowicz didn’t clarify whether this will be an exclusive marketing arrangement, but fans with good memories may recall the first footage of The Witcher 4 was presented on PS5.

With Microsoft owning a chunk of the Western RPG development scene, this seems like a shrewd move by Sony to me.

It’ll want to present CDPR’s next title as a major PlayStation title, because there’s still a possibility the next Elder Scrolls may skip its system entirely. (Although Xbox is currently saying either way.)

Obviously we’ll need to wait and see how deep this partnership runs, but if it’s anything like Sony’s GTA 6 campaign, then it’ll be very aggressive to say the least.

Arsenal v Borussia Dortmund LIVE: Kepa replaces Raya as Gyokeres starts in Champions League clash

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

The Gunners will be looking to extend their unbeaten Champions League start and close in on a top-eight finish

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Arsenal host Borussia Dortmund in the Champions League as Mikel Arteta’s side look to continue their unbeaten start, but the Gunners will do so with injured goalkeeper David Raya.

The Spaniard was forced off while holding his shoulder during Arsenal’s draw at Newcastle on Saturday but Arteta confirmed he does not need surgery in what is a huge boost to last season’s Champions League finalists.

One of Kepa Arrizabalaga and Illan Meslier will deputise as Arsenal enter a challenging stretch of fixtures that includes a meeting with Premier League rivals Manchester City on Sunday, followed by Brentford, Tottenham and Real Madrid.

Arsenal enter the week sitting third in the Champions League league phase, with three wins and a draw from their first four. Dortmund lie 12th, with three wins and one defeat, but the German side are top of the Bundesliga - two points ahead of Bayern Munich.

Follow live updates from Arsenal v Borussia Dortmund, below:

Noni Madueke has shown his quality for Arsenal in the absence of Bukayo Saka.

He scored the equaliser at the weekend to rescue a point for the Gunners against Newcastle and has been impressive in Europe.

"He is becoming decisive in games and that is what we need from players,” Mikel Arteta said.

“He is adding variety and much more connection within his game.

“I love his intensity without the ball, so he is becoming a much more complete player; I think he is enjoying this a lot."

None of Arsenal's 46 matches against German opposition has finished goalless.

The Gunners have lost only one of their last six home Champions League home games against German clubs.

Meanwhile, Dortmund have lost 11 of their last 15 games against English clubs including the most recent, 0-2 at Tottenham Hotspur on Matchday 7 last season.

All eight of the past meetings between these two teams have come in the Champions League group stage, Arsenal winning four and Dortmund three.

Arsenal have won three of their four home matches against Dortmund, losing the other 1-2 in 2013/14.

Borussia Dortmund midfielder Joey Veerman spoke about what he is expecting from tonight’s match and who his team are wary of in the Arsenal lines.

"Bruno's [Guimaraes] passing game is superb; I played against him in the Champions League,” Veerman revealed.

“[Declan] Rice is a top player; he has everything. It will be a good contest."

Plenty of English players such as Jude and Jobe Bellingham have cut their teeth in professional football at Dortmund with the German club renowned for growing and developing young talent.

"The Bundesliga is a top league, where young talent can come through, especially at Dortmund. As a club we are known for developing younger players,” said Kovac.

“The English players are very strong physically, technically at the highest level, but the Premier League is the best league in the world and they need playing time.

“A move to Germany, especially Dortmund, is a very good step."

"We are not only playing against the best defence in England and Europe, but also against the reigning English champions, Champions League finalists last season and the current Premier League leaders,” Dortmund manager Niko Kovac explained during his pre-match press conference on Monday.

“We're aware how difficult this is going to be. They have a great team and a great coach.

“But we're also difficult to play against and we'll have our chances."

"I am very impressed with them and their results so far, where they are in the [German] Bundesliga and Champions League,” Mikel Arteta said of Arsenal’s opponents tonight; Borussia Dortmund.

“The way they are playing, they way they are dominating games. We faced them in pre-season and they showed their quality and what they are capable of, so we are very much aware of that.

“But we will play at home in front of our crowd and go out to attack the game."

David Raya’s injury could have been a cause for concern for Arsenal in previous seasons but the strength in depth in the squad means there are able bodies ready to step in.

The decision that faced Mikel Arteta ahead of this game was who would replace Raya in goal; Kepa Arrizabalaga or Illan Meslier.

Arteta has made his choice but he was adamant that either needed to be prepared.

He said: "We have seen what David brings to the team but that is why we [have strength in depth] with Kepa [Arrizabalaga] and Illan [Meslier], and everybody needs to be ready because these things unfortunately happen."

Paris Saint-Germain and Real Madrid are the only teams to still have 100 per cent records after Liverpool’s defeat at Fenerbahce.

Dortmund XI: Kobel; Gadou, Schlotterbeck, Anton; Ryerson, Veerman, Bellingham, Svensson; Nmecha, Beier; Silva

Arsenal vs Borussia Dortmund latest updates

Arsenal vs Borussia Dortmund will not be broadcast live in the UK on TNT Sports. That is because under the current Champions League rights agreement on these shores, Amazon Prime Video has the first pick of one Tuesday night fixture to show exclusively on their platform up until the semi-finals.

This week they have chosen the encounter at the Emirates Stadium, which kicks off at 8pm GMT. Coverage on Amazon begins at 6:30pm, hosted by Gabby Logan.

Amazon’s current arrangement will continue from 2027-2031 as per the terms of the next Champions League TV rights deal, which will see the bulk of matches head to US streaming giants Paramount in an agreement struck in 2025 worth well over £1billion.

Unai Emery’s team has undergone a significant rebuild since that stirring night in May, with six of the starting XI who beat Freiburg sold during the summer.

Aston Villa supporters have happy memories of Istanbul after May’s Europa League final, and they return to face Galatasaray this evening in a fixture which could forecast their Champions League prospects.

It has been par for the course for Unai Emery’s team in Europe so far, with victories over Club Brugge, Fenerbahce and Viking and a defeat at Barcelona aligning with the form book.

Nine points from 12 leaves them inside the top eight at the halfway stage of the league phase, but tougher challenges are ahead with PSG and Borussia Dortmund to come after tonight’s game.

Since a statement victory over Manchester City, it has been a frustrating time for Villa either side of the international break. They were dumped out of the League Cup by Bournemouth, were second-best in a home draw with Fulham, threw away a pair of two-goal leads at Old Trafford, and were held by Sunderland at the weekend.

Though Villa put six past Galatasaray’s city rivals Fenerbahce at Villa Park, away games against Turkish opposition can be different beasts, as Liverpool discovered in the last round of Champions League matches.

Galatasaray’s two major attacking threats, Victor Osimhen and Rafael Leão, are both absent with injuries. Davinson Sanchez and Lucas Torreira are former Premier League players in their squad. Galatasaray have won two of four Champions League games, losing to Barcelona and Sporting Lisbon, but they are top of the Turkish Super Lig.

Villa will be without right-back Matty Cash owing to a concussion sustained during the international break with Poland, but will hope to have Victor Lindelof available again after he missed the Sunderland draw with illness.

Emery’s team were unable to train at Rams Park yesterday in order to give a troublesome pitch maximum recovery time after emergency renovation work.

“If we could have trained in the stadium of Galatasaray, maybe we would have decided for the same situation, but it’s not changing our routine,” Emery said. “How the pitch is going to be tomorrow is important, but it’s not an excuse because it’s [the same] for both teams. Hopefully the pitch will be as best as possible.”

Liverpool’s Florian Wirtz enjoying and relishing uptick in form

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Wirtz has struggled to settle in England since making the switch from Bayer Leverkusen but feels he his hitting form at a good time for the Reds

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Florian Wirtz admitted he has been unhappy with some of his performances for Liverpool but he is now enjoying being in his best run of form since joining the club.

The Germany playmaker’s performances have been intensely scrutinised since his £116m move from Bayer Leverkusen two summers ago and, after an underwhelming first season in a campaign where the whole team struggled, he has started to kick into gear.

He has scored five times and contributed two assists in his last four appearances for club and country and the smile is back on his face.

“In the last few games I got a lot of goal contributions and I think you could say that I am in the best form since I arrived,” he said ahead of the Champions League game against Club Brugge in Belgium.

“To be honest, I cannot really say that I have changed much. I think that I have become a bit better on a physical level, that is the only reason I could explain it.

“But I also started to know better English football and the league and I am feeling very good. I am enjoying my football at Liverpool. I am happy it is going like this at the moment.

“I have heard a lot of people talking about me smiling or that I don’t smile that much. Maybe that is only because the people don’t really know me. Normally I am a very positive guy.

“It’s just when I am on the pitch I try and bring out my best performances and be as focused as I can be. Maybe that is sometimes why it looks like I don’t smile so much, but I don’t think that is a thing you have to be concerned about.

“It’s mainly a thing that goes on in my own head. It’s not about the people who talk about me. It’s the goals I set myself and the performances I want to show for myself.

“I always believed in myself. I knew my qualities and I know them now.

“Of course, there were days where I wasn’t happy with myself but I still believe it makes no sense to put too much pressure on yourself as that doesn’t help to get to where you want.

“I just believed in myself so I wasn’t too annoyed. But now I can only speak about the time I am having now and I’m really enjoying it.”

After a run one goal in 17 club appearances stretching back to May, the 23-year-old has scored in back-to-back matches and he created 10 chances in Sunday’s win against Manchester United, including an assist for Victor Munoz’s goal.

Wirtz added: “I would say my priority is that the club and the team is successful, that’s the main thing in my head.

“I know that offensive players get checked by numbers and I’m always trying to use my opportunities when I have them but, in the end, for me it’s just about the success of the team.

“I’m not going into a game and trying to be the player who has scored the most goals or something. If I see a team-mate in a better position I give them the ball and I hope they also do that with me.”

Johan Manzambi slammed, John McGinn threatened: Four things you may have missed from Aston Villa v Galatasaray

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Correspondent AUTHORITY Football journalist with experience at Reach PLC and PA Media; University of Central Lancashire graduate. FOCUS Fan-engagement content and match analysis across the Premier League and Championship. THE INSIGHT Oliver utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified reporting and fan-first content. He provides the human stories behind the results to ensure fans get the full picture as it happens.

Johan Manzambi was among the Aston Villa stars to struggle as they were beaten 1-0 by Galatasaray in the Champions League on Tuesday.

Deniz Gul's late header sealed all three points for the hosts as Villa suffered a second loss of the Champions League campaign.

The first half was full of endeavour from both sides, but neither could muster a shot on target before the break.

Galatasaray went close through Aleksey Batrakov and Yunus Akgun in the early stages of the second half before Gul netted in the 77th minute.

The game was full of flashpoints and controversies that were lost to the intense atmosphere inside RAMS Park.

Football Insider have picked out four moments from the clash that some Villa supporters may have failed to spot.

Villa sometimes let the intimidating Istanbul atmosphere get to them in the first half, officially misplacing 26 passes as a team.

One of those nearly resulted in a Galatasaray opener, as Swiss starlet Mazambi gave the ball away to Akgun in the 20th minute, who was ultimately thwarted by Mings in the area.

Mings then stood with his arms outstretched towards Manzambi and made it clear that he was unhappy with his teammate for his sloppiness, in an unseen moment that showed just how tough the hosts made it for Villa.

The cameras initially failed to capture Jackson's 41st-minute booking, which was the first of the evening to be produced by referee Daniel Siebert.

It was quickly revealed that the Villa striker was shown a yellow for slowing down the chance for Gala's players to take a free kick after Ian Maatsen had committed a foul while trying to attack on the left wing.

Jackson threw the ball over for Davinson Sanchez to boot it upfield, but then quickly ran to kick it further back to where he believed the offence had taken place.

He then stood in front of the ball to block the free-kick and was correctly booked for delaying the restart.

It was a tense and tetchy night at RAMS Park that threatened to boil over on several occasions in the second half.

The first of those moments came in the 53rd minute when Villa captain McGinn crashed into Gala midfielder Batrakov before the pair got to their feet and clashed as others rushed in.

While they each earned bookings, replays showed that Batrakov very clearly kicked out at the Scottish star while on the floor before a scuffle ensued.

It was easy to miss and could have landed him in hot water had the officials been more diligent.

Under IFAB's new law changes, a substituted player must leave the pitch within 10 seconds at the nearest boundary line, or their replacement must wait until the next stoppage after at least one minute of play

Gomes made Siebert enforce the rule in the 66th minute as he took too long to come off for Leon Goretzka.

Villa were then forced to play with 10 men for the next 60 seconds with Gala on the attack, and will have been relieved not to go behind before they did shortly after.

Freddy Peralta lands with Orioles: After post-trade turnaround, ALCS MVP scores five-year, $125 million deal

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Veteran right-handed starter Freddy Peralta has signed with the Baltimore Orioles on a five-year deal, the team announced on Tuesday. The contract is worth $125 million with no deferrals or options, The Athletic reports.

Peralta, who will be going into his age-31 season in 2027, is coming off a campaign in which he didn't pitch up to his usual standards during a disastrous stint with the New York Mets. However, he did more like his old self following a deadline trade to the Tampa Bay Rays. On the Rays' watch, Peralta made a series of adjustments that helped him find better results on the mound, including being named the ALCS MVP.

Earlier this offseason, CBS Sports ranked Peralta as the No. 7 free agent of the 2026-27 class. Here's part of our write-up:

"It has been a roller coaster year for Peralta, who flopped with the Mets, rebounded with the Rays, then faced the Brewers (his former team) in the World Series and lost. The post-deadline stint with Tampa salvaged Peralta's free agent year. They made several adjustments, most notably raising his arm angle and getting him to pitch exclusively from the stretch, and the result was a livelier fastball and also better command. He's still five-and-fly Freddy. Even the best version of Peralta won't pitch deep into games and can be a tough watch because he nibbles and throws so many pitches per inning. They tend to be good innings, though, and the guy never misses a start. Worst case, Peralta is a mid-rotation starter you can count on to take the ball every fifth day. The best case is someone who can front a contender's rotation. Fastball Freddy was always going to be a great bounceback candidate this offseason. The Rays just beat everyone to it at the trade deadline."

Peralta isn't far removed from a 2023-25 stretch for the Milwaukee Brewers that saw him top 30 starts and 200 strikeouts in three straight seasons. In 2025, he earned his second All-Star selection and finished fifth in the National League Cy Young vote.

"Freddy has proven to be a winning player who understands what it takes to compete and succeed on the biggest stage," said Orioles president of baseball operations Mike Elias said in a statement. "His professionalism, preparation, and commitment to winning will set a strong example for our organization and we believe he will have a significant impact on our team both on and off the field."

After losing Trevor Rogers to free agency (he agreed to a five-year contract with the Chicago Cubs earlier on Tuesday), the Orioles were searching for a new frontline starter to join a rotation that includes Kyle Bradish, Shane Baz and some combination of Brandon Young, Cade Povich and Trey Gibson. Elias has been hesitant to pay up for pitchers recently, but either his strategy has changed or he decided Peralta was worth the cost.

The Orioles' disappointing fourth-place AL East finish in 2026 has raised questions about the state of Baltimore's rebuild, particularly amid stalled growth from its promising young position players, including Jackson Holliday, Gunnar Henderson and Colton Cowser. Would-be franchise backstop Adley Rutschman was traded to the Boston Red Sox at the trade deadline.

Because Peralta was traded during the 2026 season and was thus ineligible for the qualifying offer, the Orioles will not be penalized a draft pick for signing him.

Fantasy Basketball Week 5 takeaways: Wemby's historic dominance, key injuries and more from all 30 teams

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

A quick fantasy basketball breakdown on all 30 teams after Week 5 of the 2026-27 NBA season.

Spurs: Victor Wembanyama dominated Saturday: 50 points, 19 rebounds, 5 blocks and 4 steals in 32 minutes of a 114-91 win over the Pelicans. It got him the High Score of the week, with 97 fantasy points. He's just the third player since 1973-74 with a 50/15/5/4 game. It's his second 50-piece this month (52 on Nov. 4), and San Antonio is 15-3 on the season with Wemby leading the way as the top player in fantasy.

Bulls: Josh Giddey posted 23 points, 14 rebounds and a career-high 22 assists in Sunday's 151-145 OT loss to Denver, the league's first 20-20 game this season. Giddey followed up that epic game with a 2-for-11 performance with 4 points, 11 assists, 2 stocks and 3 turnovers on Monday. He was benched in crunch time (again). If you have Giddey, I'd sell. The game-to-game variance is too erratic.

Heat: Bam Adebayo posted 24 points, 15 rebounds and a career-high 12 assists with zero turnovers in Sunday's 123-113 win over OKC with Giannis Antetokounmpo (groin) out. Miami has won seven straight to reach 10-6. Shout-out to Klay Thompson, who averaged 20.7 points and 5.1 3s over a recent seven-game stretch and is closing in on Ray Allen for third all time in 3s. Keep holding Klay in 12s.

Bucks: Tyler Herro set a new career high in Philly on Saturday, dropping 50 points, then added 32 with 8 assists in Monday's win in Toronto to snap a 13-game skid. At 2-16, start prepping for some shakeups in the rotation — which could impact vets like Herro, Kyle Kuzma and Myles Turner. Rookie Brayden Burries (40% rostered) had 14 points, 10 boards, and 4 assists off the bench Monday, and should be rostered in most leagues.

Cavaliers: James Harden dropped 51 in Friday's 128-119 win over Indiana, passing Kobe Bryant for the third-most 50-point games ever (26). Evan Mobley (toe) has missed six straight and is due for re-evaluation this week. Kenny Atkinson has started Tyrese Proctor and Sam Merrill over the past week, but rookie Meleek Thomas (13.3 points in 24 minutes) is still the best streamer here.

Nets: Nolan Traore (right hamstring) will be re-evaluated in 8-10 weeks. Mikel Brown Jr. (19% rostered) got the first crack, starting Monday in Minnesota with his minutes restriction lifted: 17 points, a career-high 7 assists and 5 boards in 29 minutes. Egor Demin shot 3-for-13 off the bench. Brown is the add over Demin. The No. 6 pick has a path to 30 minutes a night.

Lakers: Austin Reaves (left heel) practiced fully Sunday and is targeting a return on Friday in San Antonio. Quentin Grimes averaged 9.2 points through 12 games, then moved into the starting lineup over the last five games, averaging 24.8 points, 4.6 rebounds, and 3.4 assists. He ranks 31st in 9-cat over that span, with 35.58 fantasy points per game..

Enjoy it now, because he won't see anywhere near 37 minutes a night when Reaves returns. Matisse Thybulle is the keeper. His 39 steals rank second in the league, and he and Wemby are the only players this year with 15+ blocks, 20+ steals and 30+ 3s.

Hawks: Atlanta has won four straight without CJ McCollum, whose left calf strain will be re-evaluated in about four weeks. No. 8 pick Kingston Flemings got next, but don't rush to get him because the Hawks won't play until Friday.

Dyson Daniels, though? He's such a unique fantasy talent. He's posting 9.5 points, 7.4 boards, 5.6 assists and a league-leading 2.9 steals per night. He can't shoot beyond 10 feet, but he's touching nearly every major counting-stats category except blocks and 3s.

Grizzlies: No. 3 pick Cameron Boozer (ankle) will be re-evaluated in four weeks. That hurts. Kris Murray started in his absence on Monday, playing 32 minutes and got 5 stocks. Add him to the watchlist, even though Memphis will likely form a committee between Murray, Taylor Hendricks and Jerami Grant (when he gets back). Make sure Cam Spencer (45% ) isn't on waivers.

Clippers: Brandon Ingram started in his debut Monday in Houston and posted 16 points, 7 boards, and 4 assists in 27 minutes. The shooting was unsurprisingly rough (6-for-18, 0-for-6 from three), but 18 shots in his first game since May is a great sign. Marc Stein reported that LA is showcasing vets like Rui Hachimura and Kris Dunn before the trade deadline.

Jazz: Svi Mykhailiuk went OFF Sunday for 39 points and 10 threes, both career highs. I'd rather pick up Ace Bailey, though.

76ers: Philly's five-game win streak ended Monday against Portland with Joel Embiid (illness) out. Embiid's been better, and fantasy managers could take the opportunity to float offers because health is always the biggest question mark with Embiid.

Celtics: Four straight losses. Derrick White is shooting 40.1% from the field and 28.8% from 3, well off his 45.4/38.7 peak. I'm holding in 9-cat, but it's been a disappointing start.

Mavericks: Just two games this week, and two scheduled before the NBA Cup next week. Cut fringe Mavs for players who play more games over the next two weeks.

Knicks: Josh Hart is averaging 6.6 points on 34/27/60 shooting. Woof.

Pistons: Over the past five games, Ausar Thompson ranks 16th in 9-cat. He's making 87.5% of his free throws (albeit low volume, but still!)

Magic: Anthony Black continues to lap Jalen Suggs. Suggs is averaging 9-5-4 with 35/29/73 shooting splits. He's down to 73% rostered, but fantasy managers should stay patient as he ramps up from a bone bruise injury that cost him eight games.

Wizards: The Wizards want Trae Young to focus less on scoring and more on passing. Per Kevin O'Connor, Young had 8 minutes of possession time per game over his first 6 games. It's down to 5 minutes over his last 5 games.

Raptors: Toronto lost to the 2-16 Bucks and sits at 6-11 overall. Kawhi Leonard (knee) is shooting at practice but is still a couple of weeks away from returning. Jakob Poeltl is back on the radar after averaging 11.0 points, 9.3 rebounds, 3.7 assists, 1.3 steals, and 1.3 blocks in 29.7 minutes per game in his last three games.

Pacers: At 5-12 without Tyrese Haliburton, Indiana is reportedly looking to make a move. In the meantime, Kelly Oubre Jr. (36%) should be rostered in all leagues.

Hornets: Brandon Miller and Coby White sat all of OT in a four-point loss to the Lakers last week. Minutes restrictions are costing teams Ws.

Timberwolves: Rudy Gobert ranks 114th in points leagues and 290th in 9-cat. His efficiency near the rim is pacing to his worst since his rookie season — he's 17-for-53 on layups through Monday. Plus, his rebounding and block rates are tumbling. Keep an eye on Joan Beringer.

Rockets: At 13-5, Houston owns the NBA's best offensive rating (121.1) and a plus-8.2 net rating (5th in the league).

Nuggets: Nikola Jokić logged triple-double No. 202 on Sunday. He's seven back of Russell Westbrook's record.

Trail Blazers: Donovan Clingan is finishing 74% at the rim, up from 66% last season.

Thunder: Nearly 80% of Ajay Mitchell's shots are 2s and he's hitting 28.3% from 3. He'll need to extend that range if the Thunder want to right the ship.

Suns: Four straight wins after Monday's OT victory in New Orleans. If Jalen Green was dropped in a points league, pick him up.

Warriors: Stephen Curry (oblique) is due for re-evaluation this week.

Pelicans: New Orleans sits at 5-14, so it's a team to watch by the deadline. Keep holding the young guys, Jeremiah Fears and Derik Queen. Everyone else could be on the block.

Kings: Keegan Murray has 12-plus points and seven-plus boards in four of his last five. Rounding into form? Maybe.

Ref Watch: Wild moment among three Daniel Siebert shockers in Aston Villa v Galatasaray

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Correspondent AUTHORITY Football journalist with experience at Reach PLC and PA Media; University of Central Lancashire graduate. FOCUS Fan-engagement content and match analysis across the Premier League and Championship. THE INSIGHT Oliver utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified reporting and fan-first content. He provides the human stories behind the results to ensure fans get the full picture as it happens.

Daniel Siebert refereed poorly as Aston Villa were beaten 1-0 by Galatasaray in the Champions League on Tuesday.

Deniz Gul's late header sealed all three points for the hosts as Villa suffered a second loss of the Champions League campaign.

The first half was full of endeavour from both sides, but neither could muster a shot on target before the break.

Galatasaray went close through Aleksey Batrakov and Yunus Akgun in the early stages of the second half before Gul netted in the 77th minute

German official Daniel Siebert was the man in the middle of a heated Champions League affair in Istanbul.

Football Insider have picked out three calls that Siebert should look back on and regret from the RAMS Park clash.

UEFA follow the laws of the game set by IFAB in its competitions, whose rulebook clearly states that players must be sanctioned if they excessively gesture towards the referee for an opponent to be shown a card.

That is exactly what Galatasaray forward Yilmaz did in the 12th minute of the game when he was fouled by Villa defender Victor Lindelof.

The Turkish international was captured on camera pleading to Siebert for Lindelof to be booked by making the gesture, yet the referee went against the laws and did not show Yilmaz the yellow.

The tense nature of the game nearly blew up in Siebert's face as players from both sides clashed in the 53rd minute following a challenge between John McGinn and Batrakov.

That was preceded by a wild lunge from ex-Tottenham defender Sanchez on young Villa winger Alysson, as his foot clipped the top of the ball but also caught the Brazilian while he tried to advance in the final third.

Siebert strangely let play continue and did not even book the Colombian centre-back, which was a clear mistake in itself and looked even worse when another clash seconds later caused a scuffle between the two teams.

Torreira certainly had a reputation for rash tackling and a hot-headed nature among English football fans during his previous spell at Arsenal.

It was clear to see that nothing has changed in that regard, based on Tuesday's clash, as the Uruguayan made several poor challenges and displayed some clear gamesmanship.

He committed four fouls on the night, one of which was a clear booking as he tackled Joao Gomes late and from behind in the 33rd minute but escaped without a sanction.

Torreira committed his second bookable offence late on as he clearly moved his arm towards the ball when Ibrahim Mbaye aimed to put Villa on the attack with a chipped pass.

Siebert gave a free-kick for that incident but somehow did not book the Gala enforcer, who can count himself lucky that he was able to be substituted off in added-time.

Villa beaten late on at Galatasaray as winless run continues

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Galatasaray vs Aston Villa. UEFA Champions League Round 1.

Match report as Deniz Gul's strike beats Aston Villa in the Champions League league phase, with Galatasaray winning 1-0; the game lacked chances, with Villa failing to score for a second game in a row; Unai Emery's side have not won in six matches

Aston Villa's winless run extended to six games as a late Deniz Gul's header saw them beaten 1-0 by Galatasaray in the Champions League league phase.

It is the first time Villa have lost back-to-back league phase or group games under Unai Emery, putting them in a perilous position ahead of tough games against PSG and Borussia Dortmund in their next two European fixtures.

The Premier League side have not won since beating Manchester City on October 24, and have not scored since Leon Goretzka's strike against Manchester United on November 8.

It was a game that lacked chances, and it took until the 79th minute - Gul's goal - for a shot on target. Villa failed to deal with a corner, with Nicolas Jackson's poor attempted clearance landing to the Galatasaray forward, who nodded home.

He took a boot to the face from Jackson with the Villa forward attempting to clear. There was a VAR check for a possible red card, but nothing was given.

In contrast to Emery's side, Galatasaray extend their winning run to eight games in all competitions ahead of the Istanbul derby against Besiktas on Monday. They move up to 13th in the league phase table, and have now won three successive Champions League games - a club record.

Aston Villa remain in the top eight, but with the majority of the matchday five fixtures yet to be played. They play away to Ipswich next, live on Saturday Night Football.

23: OFFSIDE! Nicolas Jackson finds Johan Manzambi with a cute pass, which the latter fires straight at Cakur. However, the flag is up after.

32: CLOSE! Yunus Akgun delivers a great cross, but no one can convert.

52: CONFRONTATION! John McGinn and Aleksey Batrakov square up after the Villa captain barged the Galatasaray forward to the floor. It came after a reckless high challenge from Davinson Sanchez on Alysson.

64: CLOSE! Gul nods the ball back into the box after a great cross in, but Villa clear.

79: GOAL! Gul nods home after some poor defending from Villa, as VAR checks and clears a possible red card for Jackson.

Gasperini: ‘A great moment for Roma to face the best’ ahead of PSG test

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Gian Piero Gasperini says Roma’s trip to Paris Saint-Germain is “a great moment” to test themselves against the best, and admits the Lisbon defeat with Atalanta still leaves a bitter taste.

With quotes via TuttoMercatoWeb, the Roma coach was speaking at his pre-match press conference at the Parc des Princes, alongside Paulo Dybala, ahead of Wednesday’s Champions League clash. Kick-off is at 21:00 CET [20:00 GMT].

“It’s definitely a very, very good moment for us to be able to play this game against the strongest team,” Gasperini said. “What we’re thinking about most at the moment is putting in a high-level performance.

“It’s clear we have to measure ourselves against this team. It’s the first time we’ve faced them with Roma, so we’ll have to understand straight away on the pitch what their level is. It’s certainly very high, and we need to see how much we can counter their technique and their speed. Our aim is to reduce their effectiveness a little so that we can also play our own game.”

Roma go to Paris with four points from four games after dramatic defeats to Real Madrid and Manchester United. PSG, the holders, have won all four of theirs.

Gasperini insisted those losses shouldn’t affect his side’s confidence.

“This is what the Champions League is like. It’s the most important competition in the world, with the highest level,” he said. “You can put in very good performances, like we did against Real and Manchester, but then there are key moments, and the best players in the world are on the pitch and can decide games.

“We have to focus on producing that type of performance, because it gives you confidence, makes you grow and carries over into the league.”

This is not Gasperini’s first big night against PSG. In the 2020 quarter-final in Lisbon, his Atalanta side were leading going into the final minutes, but lost 2-1 to two late goals.

“That’s a game that’s still a bit hard to swallow, because of the ending,” he admitted. “We were down to 10 men in the final minutes because of Freuler’s injury and had no substitutions left. PSG brought on Mbappé, and he was decisive. But it’s still one of the highest moments in Atalanta’s history.

“This is a different type of game, because PSG are different. They’re much more of a team now, and that’s thanks to Luis Enrique. Roma are coming into this competition after several years away, which is different for us. It’s a league-phase game, and for us it’s a measure of what we’re doing.”

Newcastle United, Aston Villa and Everton are reportedly among the Premier League clubs interested in signing Cruzeiro defender Jonathan Jesus ahead of the January transfer window, as competition for the Brazilian youngster intensifies.

The 22-year-old has attracted considerable attention following an impressive campaign in Brazil, where his performances have caught the eye of several clubs across Europe.

According to a TEAMtalk report, Newcastle, Aston Villa and Everton are among nine Premier League sides monitoring the centre-back’s situation.

Newcastle could view the Brazilian as an exciting addition to their defensive department, particularly as they assess potential reinforcements ahead of the second half of the season.

Aston Villa are also keeping tabs on the Cruzeiro defender, with Unai Emery potentially looking to add further quality and depth to his defensive options. Everton, meanwhile, are among the clubs interested in securing his services as they consider strengthening their backline.

Brighton, Bournemouth, Brentford, Nottingham Forest, Sunderland and Fulham are the other English clubs tracking the youngster. Some sides have reportedly sent scouts to watch him during the recent international break, while others have contacted intermediaries to understand the financial requirements of a potential transfer.

Jesus has made more than 40 appearances for Cruzeiro this year and recently earned his first senior Brazil call-up under Carlo Ancelotti. He debuted against Japan before earning a starting opportunity in Brazil’s emphatic 7-0 victory.

The Premier League clubs will face competition from abroad, with Portuguese giants Benfica and Porto also interested in the defender. Clubs in France and Spain, alongside Shakhtar Donetsk, are monitoring his development.

Cruzeiro are under no immediate pressure to sell, with Jesus contracted until June 2029.

However, growing interest in his services could make January a key period for his future, particularly if Newcastle, Villa or Everton decide to formalise their interest.

Journalist confirms Liverpool have been 'in contact' with €70m midfield sensation

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Andoni Iraola finally seems to be turning things around at Liverpool.

His squad are just coming off the back of a 4-0 win against arch-rivals Manchester United which proved how good they can be.

Indeed, the Red Devils did end up with a red card within the first 10 minutes of the clash but, the football that Liverpool played on Sunday was some of their best of the season.

If they continue performing like that, there's a good chance that they could get themselves back into the race for the Premier League title.

At the moment, seven points separate them and first-placed Arsenal and, while that's still quite a big gap, it's a number that they can start hacking away at fairly easily.

This task would be made an awful lot easier if, during the January transfer window, Liverpool manage to add one or two new faces to their ranks.

Liverpool have been 'in contact' with Lamine Camara

Lamine Camara is one of the main players who was linked with Liverpool in the summer.

The Senegalese midfielder was rumoured to be one of the Reds' priority targets with them, alongside many other clubs vying for his transfer, despite his €70 million price tag.

Liverpool were the first ones to be linked with the 22-year-old but, when all the other clubs entered the race, the deal got too messy and in the end, they pulled out.

However, there were some rumours on transfer deadline day which claimed that Liverpool had told AS Monaco and Camara to wait for them until January.

And, to the surprise of many, they did just that and pulled out of their deal with Chelsea at the last minute.

The ending of negotiations led to many different questions being asked and several rumours being shared but now, David Lynch, while speaking to Anfield Index, has confirmed that "contact" has been held between the two parties.

"Liverpool did have a word with his representatives and just said kind of hang on for us," he said.

"I’ve got much more solid information that he’s someone that they really like.

"[He is] a player that they like that they’ve been in contact with."

Liverpool must decide who they want their next central midfielder to be

As of now, nobody knows exactly what Liverpool's plans for the January transfer window are.

Many different rumours have been shared with fans, pundits and journalists all making up their own theories.

But, only those within the Reds' recruitment department will know exactly what lies ahead.

However, if their plans don't include a new central midfielder, some serious questions will need to be asked.

Many different players have emerged as options for Liverpool, and, if a new one is on their shortlist, they need to figure out who it's going to be.

Whether it's Camara, Scott, Wharton or even an out there option like Gavi, a decision needs to be made quickly.
