Mauricio Pochettino has already had one wish granted as Manchester United manager

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Pochettino has already had one of his first wishes granted as Manchester United manager.

The Argentine arrived in the north west on Wednesday, immediately boarding a plane from Cincinnati after his final match in charge of the United States national team to begin work at Carrington as soon as he touched down.

The former Tottenham and Chelsea head coach takes his new side to Anfield this afternoon to face Liverpool in a tricky first match at the helm.

The 54-year-old revealed in his introductory press conference on Friday that he wanted input in big decisions at United as his tenure wore on, and he has already had one particular request granted in his short spell in the hotseat.

In a round of quickfire questions with the club’s media team upon his arrival, Pochettino was asked whether he preferred team lineups to be formatted in shirt number or position order, with clubs having differing approaches to their pre-match graphics.

United have traditionally plumped for a shirt number-first approach, but when Pochettino’s first team selection was revealed ahead of the game against Liverpool, the Red Devils’ first XI was listed in position order.

It outlined that their new boss had gone for a classic 4-2-3-1 shape, listing Leny Yoro and Diogo Dalot as full-backs, and indicating that Matheus Cunha would play up front rather than Bryan Mbeumo.

United travel to Merseyside this afternoon mired in the Premier League relegation zone, winning just once in their last 11 games.

It was a streak that lost Michael Carrick his job, with the former midfielder sacked a week and a half ago after 303 days in charge.

Marc Marquez overtakes Valentino Rossi - now only Angel Nieto and Giacomo Agostini have more titles

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Marc Marquez continues to cement his legacy in motorcycle racing and sport as a whole. If his return to the top in 2025, with an impeccable season until he was injured, already left everyone stunned, the legendary Cervera rider has pushed his limits once again in 2026, overcoming every obstacle thrown his way to claim his 10th world title, and his eighth in MotoGP.

This is a tally that allows the Spaniard to continue breaking historical records. While many already consider him the greatest of all time, the #93 continues to strengthen his case with the numbers to back it up. And with the crown he secured this weekend at the Portuguese Grand Prix, he has reached milestones that many considered impossible in the modern era.

One of those is the number of motorcycle racing world titles across all categories. Marquez has reached the landmark of 10 championships, moving ahead of several legends of the sport.

With his 10 titles, Marquez has finally overtaken Valentino Rossi, who finished his career with nine, with his best opportunity to surpass that tally coming in 2015, when he ultimately lost out to Jorge Lorenzo amid the controversy surrounding Marc. Not only has Marquez surpassed the Doctor's tally, but also that of Mike 'The Bike' Hailwood, who won nine titles in the 1960s, and Italian Carlo Ubbiali, who achieved the same feat in the 1950s.

As a result, Marquez now has only two legendary figures ahead of him: Angel Nieto and Giacomo Agostini. Nieto's '12+1' world titles appeared untouchable until just a few years ago, but the reality is that Marquez is now just three championships short of the man regarded as the father of Spanish motorcycle racing.

It is worth remembering that Marquez still has at least two more years on his contract with Ducati, meaning he could potentially get very close to Nieto's tally. However, catching Agostini at the top of the all-time list, with his formidable 15 world titles, will be a much tougher task.

It should also be noted that during the eras of these great champions, it was common for riders to compete in more than one category in the same season, which explains the higher championship tallies. By contrast, legends such as Marquez and Rossi, as well as more recent champions with fewer titles, like Mick Doohan and Jorge Lorenzo, have competed primarily in a single class each season.

The picture changes considerably if we consider only world titles won in the premier class (500cc initially, followed by MotoGP from 2002). In that list, Marquez is already among the most successful riders in history, tied at the top with Giacomo Agostini. If Marquez wins another MotoGP title, he will become the most successful rider in the history of the premier class.

8 MotoGP titles  (2013, 2014, 2016-2019, 2025 and 2026)

2 500cc titles  (2001 and 2002), 5 MotoGP titles (2003-2005, 2008 and 2009)

4 500cc titles (1984, 1986, 1988 and 1989)

Liverpool vs Manchester United LIVE: Pochettino names team for first game as Premier League rivals face off

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The headline act of this weekend’s Premier League action sees Mauricio Pochettino handed a baptism of fire at Anfield. The former Tottenham and Chelsea manager is back in the English top flight, becoming the latest man tasked with guiding Man Utd back to the top of the game after replacing Michael Carrick on a two-and-a-half-year deal.

Liverpool first up is a less-than-ideal start for Pochettino, who started his new role just four days ago and took charge of training for the first time just three days ago, footage of which was posted - and then deleted - off YouTube after being inadvertently captured by a drone. Marcus Rashford was then ruled out due to an ongoing injury issue.

So, it’s not been the best preparation for one of Man Utd’s biggest games of the season, while Liverpool have been boosted by the return to fitness of three stars, including captain Virgil van Dijk. Follow the game LIVE below with our dedicated match blog, featuring expert insight and analysis as Pochettino begins life at Man Utd.

Calvin Bassey has confirmed he is in talks over a new contract with Fulham, amid interest from Manchester United.

As reported by Standard Sport earlier this week, 26-year-old centre-back Bassey, was expected to be a transfer target for Mauricio Pochettino in the January transfer window.

Defence is a key area to strengthen for Man Utd, and Fulham captain Bassey has an admirer in Pochettino.

But Bassey, who is in the third year of a four-year contract at Fulham, is happy in west London and ready to commit his future to the club.

Mauricio Pochettino has already had one of his first wishes as Manchester United manager.

The 54-year-old revealed in his introductory press conference on Friday that he wanted input in big decisions at United as his tenure wore on, and he has already had one particular request granted in his short spell in the hotseat.

In a round of quickfire questions with the club’s media team upon his arrival, Pochettino was asked whether he preferred team lineups to be formatted in shirt number or position order, with clubs having differing approaches to their pre-match graphics.

United have traditionally plumped for a shirt number-first approach, but when Pochettino’s first team selection was revealed ahead of the game against Liverpool, the Red Devils’ first XI was listed in position order.

Andoni Iraola has sprung a surprise on his left flank too, with Cody Gakpo out of the matchday squad and Rio Ngumoha in from the off.

The 18-year-old is ahead of Milos Kerkez, while Bradley Barcola lines up on the right, against Dalot.

Alexander Isak is up top with Florian Wirtz tucked in behind him, and Dominik Szoboszlai is fit enough to start alongside Alexis Mac Allister.

Virgil van Dijk is back to full fitness, and Ronald Araujo gets the nod at right-back ahead of the returning Jeremie Frimpong.

As expected from the Argentine, to be honest. Not much he can really do with that crop of players.

Senne Lammens in goal, and an interesting choice to bring Matthijs de Ligt straight in for Harry Maguire, but it is full-back that is still the real problem area with Luke Shaw still injured.

Diogo Dalot is at left-back once more and Leny Yoro fills in on the right once more.

In the engine room, Kobbie Mainoo and Youri Tielemans get the nod with Andrey Santos still struggling for starts, while Patrick Dorgu is the go-to left winger in the absence of Marcus Rashford.

Matheus Cunha leads the line, with Joshua Zirkzee still forced to settle for a place on the bench despite the change in manager.

Bruno Fernandes will lead Manchester United this afternoon, but talks over a new contract have ended after two rejected offers.

The Man Utd skipper has explained the reasoning behind his decision to Sky Sports ahead of today’s game.

"Yeah, the club offered me a new contract, so I think that, from their side, shows that they want me to stay,” he said.

"We spoke about the contract at the end of last season, we came in the beginning of this season speaking about the contract, but about the context of the team and the club, I just put that to a side.

“I don't think it's the right moment, neither for me, nor for the club, to be talking about negotiations because the most important thing now is that the club gets back to win games."

Liverpool manager Andoni Iraola expects a much tougher test from Manchester United following the appointment of Mauricio Pochettino.

But Iraola expects the new face in the opposition dugout to have his charges firing on all cylinders, despite limited time implementing new tactics.

Writing in his programme notes for Sunday's game, Iraola wrote: “Liverpool versus Manchester United can only ever create a sense of anticipation, no matter when and where the game is played and no matter who is involved.

“For us it is a privilege and it is one that comes with a lot of responsibility because of what this rivalry means. The eyes of the world will be on us and we should use this as a motivation.

“We want – and will almost certainly need – to be as near to our best as we can. That is the standard of this fixture.

“What people think the league table tells us in this moment is not something that we can afford to be distracted by. This is a day when this kind of thing does not matter in the same way that it might otherwise. The history of the rivalry between our two clubs tells us that.

“We should expect and prepare for the best version of United because they will definitely see this fixture as an opportunity to start showing the best of themselves, particularly under new management.”

It’s been a whirlwind start to life at Man Utd for Pochettino, which even included his first training session being inadvertently spied on by a drone operator.

The operator, behind the YouTube account CC Overview, filmed around 12 minutes of footage at Carrington on Thursday, showing Pochettino working with his new squad ahead of Sunday’s Premier League trip to Liverpool.

It has led to suggestions on social media that Pochettino’s surprise starting line-up at Anfield has been leaked, with out-of-favour Joshua Zirkzee starting up top and Bryan Mbeumo playing in a wing-back role.

English Journalist Ranks 25 Greatest Footballers He’s Seen Live

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

English football journalist John Cross has taken on the impossible task of ranking the greatest footballers he's seen in person, and his list of 25 players is bound to cause a debate.

For any fan, ranking players is a matter of preference, with factors such as playing style, achievements, position and even nationality all coming into play. You'd be hard-pressed to find any Argentine who ranks Cristiano Ronaldo above Lionel Messi.

Cross curated his 25-man list via Mirror Sport by explaining that he's been reporting on football for more than 25 years and, as such, there are many legendary names included along with modern-day stars. Where he ranks the current generation may surprise readers, though it's worth noting he deemed England's 3-2 win over Mexico at the World Cup to be the nation's 'best ever away performance'.

In 25th place is AC Milan hero Marco van Basten, a player who won three Ballons d'Or, though as Cross mentions, he had his career cut short by injuries. The Daily Mirror writer suggests that if not for injuries, he'd have made the top five on this list.

Roberto Carlos is next up, and Cross deemed him a 'brilliant entertainer' and a player who could get you out of your seat, even playing as a left-back. For many fans, this is a fair ranking of the Brazil and Real Madrid icon because there aren't many better players who have played in his position.

The first Premier League name to make Cross's list is Patrick Vieira, with the journalist particularly praising his physicality and leadership. Dubbed 'one of the greatest captains and midfielders to ever grace the English game', the former Arsenal skipper's dominance is hard to deny.

CROSS CONTROVERSY: There was no place for Patrick Vieira's longtime rival Roy Keane in his list.

Paolo Maldini is one of the players you could rank above Carlos on the all-time left-back list, and the Italian icon sits above him on Cross's list. The AC Milan great's peak came during the journalist's childhood, and he recalled watching him during the 1980s and Channel 4's coverage of Serie A.

Another Premier League star, Kevin De Bruyne, earned his place among Cross's favourites. The former Manchester City midfielder was named 'the best player in the best team in English football', though Cross noted that it comes 'with a caveat now' due to the club being found guilty of breaching financial rules.

Kevin De Bruyne, Steven Gerrard, and Patrick Vieira are all among the best midfielders in the league's history.

Sticking with playmaking gems, Cross ranked Luka Modric 20th, branding him 'the epitome of a European midfielder' and 'the greatest Croatian player in history'. He did, however, point out the Real Madrid legend's lack of understanding that 'football's coming home' is a 'self-deprecating joke at ourselves'.

In 19th place is David Beckham, maybe the first name that will stir up controversy, whether that be because he's too high or too low. There aren't many more polarising players in history because some use his celebrity status against him, while others, like Cross, believe he approached the ball like a 'work of art'.

Another Englishman, Frank Lampard, takes 18th, and Cross highlighted his goalscoring consistency along with his mindset to work 'so hard' to turn himself into 'one of the world's best'. He attributed Chelsea's most successful period to the highest-scoring midfielder in top-flight history.

Cross insisted that 'we have to have a wayward hero in there' and claimed that Eric Cantona 'absolutely fits that bill'. Citing his entertainment value, he described the former French striker as creating 'magic stardust' that helped Manchester United end their wait for a title and dominate that era of the Premier League.

It's clear who Cross sides with in the Lampard vs Steven Gerrard vs Paul Scholes debate as he chose Gerrard in 16th, omitting Scholes from his entire list. He mentioned that the former Liverpool captain's long-range goals and passion made him one of his favourite players, recalled his 2005 Champions League win and said that he 'deserved to win more trophies'.

Thierry Henry, Pep Guardiola, Deco and Cesc Fabregas are among the players that have answered the age-old Scholes vs Gerrard vs Lampard debate

Now we get to the more contentious picks, and names like Wayne Rooney falling outside of the top 10 might upset one or two fans. Cross recalled Rooney's Euro 2004 campaign, calling him 'unstoppable' and a 'street footballer' whose intelligence on and off the pitch doesn't get enough credit.

For some, Mohamed Salah isn't an entertaining player to watch, especially in comparison with the likes of Eden Hazard when that conversation comes up. For Cross, his dribbling ability and success at Liverpool make him a 'clear choice' for his list, deeming him a 'fabulous Premier League legend'.

There's a reason Cross asked Arsenal fans for their thoughts, as many regard Thierry Henry as the greatest Premier League player of all time. Given that this is the main competition he covers, it's somewhat surprising the Frenchman doesn't make the top 10, although he does applaud his mentality and natural ability, calling him one of the best he's seen on a weekly basis.

Cross views Dennis Bergkamp as the greatest Arsenal player he's seen, which is why the Dutchman sits above Henry. His admiration of Bergkamp is made clear when he acknowledges him as 'a game-changer for English football' and cites his finish against Newcastle United as being 'from another world'.

Just missing out on the top 10 is Barcelona icon Xavi, who played in a team Cross deemed to be 'the best team in Europe'. There's less analysis of the Spaniard than any other player on his list, as Cross instead chose to focus on Pep Guardiola's team rather than explain Xavi's ranking.

Kicking off Cross's top 10 is Andres Iniesta, whom he referenced in his verdict on Xavi. Between the duo, he argues that Iniesta edges it because of his passing vision and control. He called the 2010 World Cup winner 'just wonderful' and 'a joy to watch'.

Perhaps a more controversial pick is Zlatan Ibrahimovic in ninth, although Cross justified this selection by alluding to the change in opinion of the Swedish striker among English media and fans. He recalled his performance for Sweden in a win over England when he scored 'one of the greatest goals of all time' and believes he is 'one of football's greatest-ever characters'.

Kylian Mbappe is ranked eighth, and for the older generation, that is a perfectly acceptable ranking for a player who has been a revelation but has still yet to win a Ballon d'Or. For the younger generation, it might seem somewhat low because of his dominance during the modern era, winning the World Cup at the age of 19.

The 2026 Ballon d’Or winner will be announced on Monday night in London, England.

Meanwhile, a Ballon d'Or winner who did make Cross's top 10 is Harry Kane, and the journalist thinks we haven't fully appreciated the England captain for many years. Alluding to his goalscoring exploits in recent times at Bayern Munich, he claims that the former Tottenham striker has got even better with age.

Ranking Jude Bellingham sixth is Cross's most daring decision, and he defended this by tipping the Real Madrid star to be the best English player of all time. He references England's World Cup win over Mexico, describing the Birmingham-born midfielder's performance as 'amazing' and calling him 'elegant, driven and just wonderful'.

CROSS CONTROVERSY: Jude Bellingham is the highest ranked English player on the list at the age of 23.

The reaction in the comments section suggests Bellingham's spot is the most controversial call. Placing the 'truly beautiful, iconic and inspirational' Zinedine Zidane above him in fifth isn't as bold, and Cross mentions watching the French midfielder 'own the whole pitch' in a random Serie A game for Juventus, possessing 'the best control of any player' he's seen.

Zidane's former Real Madrid teammate Ronaldo Nazario takes fourth spot, a player whom Cross says 'had everything', including 'skill, power and pace'. He reckons that at his peak, he was 'the best player in the world'.

CROSS CONTROVERSY: There's no place for Ronaldo Nazario's former Brazilian teammate Ronaldinho on this entire list.

Cross admits that Cristiano Ronaldo's recent 'diva carry-ons' (his unceremonious Portugal retirement) have put him off. He still acknowledges his 'eye for the spectacular', arguing that since he left Manchester United for Real Madrid in 2009, he isn't sure English football has possessed a player of his calibre.

Lineker thinks four players in football history are greater than Cristiano Ronaldo.

Taking the runners-up medal in Cross's list is Diego Maradona, though he interestingly claims that 'it's not going to be popular'. The highly reputed journalist calls the late Argentine icon 'unstoppable' and celebrates his 1986 World Cup exploits, claiming that he won Argentina the tournament.

'The greatest dribbler and showman' Cross has ever seen, Lionel Messi, is the best player he has watched in person, and that's often the case for fans who witness the Argentine hero in action. He even reveals that he used to side with Cristiano Ronaldo in the GOAT debate, but deems Messi's 2022 World Cup heroics to have ended that discussion.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Bayern CEO Dreesen responds to fan criticism over Musiala situation

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Dreesen was questioned by Bayern fans over the club's approach to the Germany international, who continues to work towards a return to full fitness following his prolonged absence.

Acknowledging the supporters' concerns, the Bayern chief insisted that the club must give Musiala the necessary time to recover while also recognising its responsibilities towards the player and the wider organisation.

"I acknowledge your comments. I've also spoken publicly about the matter myself. I tried to interpret Uli's words, and he handled the situation sensitively. We are giving Jamal the time he needs, and we have to do so," Dreesen explained.

The 59-year-old added that his previous comments were not intended to create further discussion, but rather to underline his responsibility to protect the player's interests.

"In my role, I have a duty of care towards the player, and I tried to make it clear that Jamal will be given the time he needs to get back to his old self," he continued.

However, Dreesen also acknowledged that Bayern face a difficult situation in balancing Musiala's recovery with the club's sporting responsibilities, describing the circumstances as something football has rarely encountered before.

"Jamal's sporting future is extremely important; he is an extraordinary player. But there is a second responsibility, not just towards the player, but also towards the club," he said.

"It's like in any company when someone is no longer able to perform all of their duties. This is a new situation in football, a situation that we have to learn how to deal with."

Dreesen also referred to the unusual nature of Musiala's ongoing health situation, asking supporters to show understanding as Bayern navigate unfamiliar territory.

"These absence seizures have never occurred in elite sport, particularly in football, before. I ask for your understanding on this," the Bayern CEO concluded.

Addressing the supporter who raised the issue, Dreesen also acknowledged the message behind the criticism, remarking: "Your message is: 'Speak, but speak a bit more quietly.' I think everyone has understood that."

Former 'Orange Crush' star Tom Jackson dies at age 75

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Former Denver Broncos linebacker Tom Jackson, member of the famed 'Orange Crush' defense of the 1970's, passed away at the age of 75, the team announced on Sunday morning.

Jackson was selected by the Broncos in the fourth round of the 1973 NFL draft (88th overall) out of Louisville. Jackson only played in eight games his rookie year, but became a long-time contributor for Denver. Jackson ultimately played in 191 career games, third-most all-time in team history (177 starts) over 14 years, recording 20 interceptions (13th-best in team history), returning three picks for touchdowns, 1,531 tackles (935 solo), 44 sacks (10th most in team history) and eight fumble recoveries. Jackson was voted to three Pro Bowls, and a two-time All-Pro. Jackson was with the Denver during their first Super Bowl run in 1977, and was around when the Broncos became one of the most dominant teams of the 1980's, playing in an additional Super Bowl in 1986, his final year. Jackson became a Ring of Fame honoree in 1992.

After Jackson's playing career, he was an analyst for ESPN, teaming up with Chris Berman in breaking down the best action in the NFL.

Jackson's importance during the Orange Crush years were not forgotten. When linebacker Randy Gradishar became the first Pro Football Hall of Fame inductee from the unit, Jackson presented Gradishar for enshrinement. Sadly, Jackson never lived to see his bust in Canton.

"The Denver Broncos are heartbroken by the passing of Ring of Fame linebacker Tom Jackson, a cornerstone of the legendary Orange Crush defense and one of the most beloved players in franchise history," the team said in a statement on Sunday morning. "An inspirational leader and fierce competitor, TJ was a proud member of the Broncos' first Super Bowl team that helped establish a winning tradition in Denver. Off the field, Tom carried himself with class, warmth and humility, forging deep connections and lifelong friendships with teammates and throughout Broncos Country. TJ's dynamic personality, quick wit and football knowledge made him a natural for television and paved the way for a Hall of Fame broadcasting career. For generations of fans, Tom was an iconic fixture on ESPN's Sunday NFL Countdown and NFL Primetime, sharing his thoughtful insight with an unmistakable sense of humor that defined an era of sports broadcasting. Above all, TJ loved the Broncos and was a cherished member of our family for more than 50 years. We extend our deepest sympathies to Tom's wife, Jennifer, his daughters, Taylor and Morgan, and all of his family, friends and teammates."

The Broncos released several heartfelt tributes of Jackson, which can be seen below.

ESPN's Sunday NFL Countdown crew also wore orange ties on Sunday morning in Jackson's honor.

Maybe one day, the NFL as a whole will recognize Jackson's contributions in NFL history enough to enshrine him in the Pro Football Hall of Fame one day.

Rest in peace, Tom. Your legacy won't be forgotten.

‘Standards needed to be raised' – New Crawley Town defender gives 'brutally honest' assessment following Fleetwood win

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Experienced Heneghan came straight into Kevin Blackwell’s starting XI in the 2-1 win against Fleetwood Town on Saturday (November 21), playing as one of three centre-backs in a 3-5-2 formation.

And he repaid the new permanent manager’s faith with a dominant aerial display.

Following the game, Heneghan said: “Standards needed to be raised, I’ll be brutally honest, and as time goes on we’ll get those standards better and better.

“We’ve just got to keep working and sticking together. It’s a good group and the more we play together we’ll win some points.”

The 33 year-old’s performance was an example of driving the Reds’ standards forward from the back.

He won more than his fair share of headers and set the tone inside the first 10 minutes with a sliding block to stop a fierce Fleetwood strike from range in their first effort on goal.

Speaking about the importance of breaking their Broadfield Stadium hoodoo and earning a crucial first three points at home in the league, Heneghan said: “For the group it’s great, obviously they [Crawley] won against Oldham as well so it’s back-to-back wins in the league, which is great for confidence and morale and building on that really.”

Reflecting on his first league match in six months, the former Fleetwood player added: “Personally, it’s massive just to be back out there playing football and the relief of getting three points, so we’ve got to build on that.

“It’s the best, it’s all I’ve known since I started playing at the age of four. So, I want to continue for as long as possible until the body doesn’t work.”

Blackwell’s Reds remain unbeaten in his opening three games since taking over the reigns and singled out the performances of Heneghan, Pemi Aderoju and Danilo Orsi in Town’s first league victory since March this year.

Speaking in the post-match press conference, Blackwell said: “I thought Ben Heneghan came in, his first game for six months – I thought he did terrific. I thought [it was] maybe Pemi’s (Aderoju) best game since I’ve been here.

“[Danilo] Orsi – he’s worked so hard in training and I thought, today, he covered every blade of ground. I thought they all did well. It looked a very good unit today.

“But I want more. I need more, from certain aspects of the game and we’re hopefully trying to develop that now.”

Heneghan is only focused on the Reds’ future performances on the pitch and made it clear that the squad understands the manager’s individual and collective expectations.

He said: “What’s happened has happened and it’s gone now. The manager’s committed until the end of the season so that’s reassuring for everyone. Everyone knows their role and everyone knows what part they’re going to play.”

Crawley welcome second-placed Barnet to Broadfield on Tuesday night (November 24) and have the chance to climb out of the relegation zone with another win.

Philly coach Nick Sirianni surely has done all he can to have his team ready to play (and help save his job). The SportsLine model has the hosts covering the spread in 54% of its simulations.

Doue to undergo surgery after ACL injury

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Paris Saint-Germain confirmed Desire Doue has ruptured his anterior cruciate ligament (ACL) in his right knee and will undergo surgery.

Doue was on target as PSG registered a 3-2 win over Nice in Ligue 1 on Saturday but was then withdrawn before the break after appearing to twist his right knee awkwardly in a challenge with Sofiane Diop.

Luis Enrique confirmed after the match that it was a "serious injury" and PSG have said Doue will undergo surgery before the end of November.

No timeline has been set for his return yet, but some reports suggest he could miss six months, which would see him miss the end of PSG's season.

He would also miss France's Nations League quarter-final against Spain in March.

Communiqué médical concernant Désiré Doué. https://t.co/IQFSmjMvT1 @Aspetar https://t.co/EdvrjMnssX

— Paris Saint-Germain (@PSG_inside) November 22, 2026

Doue has been an influential figure for PSG this season with 10 direct goal involvements (six goals, four assists) in all competitions – only Ousmane Dembele (11) has more.

The 21-year-old also leads all PSG players for chances created (31) and total shots (45), while he is averaging a goal every 160.8 minutes.

He will now, however, join long-term absentee Khvicha Kvaratskhelia, who is also out with an ACL injury, in the treatment room.

Broncos Ring of Fame linebacker and Hall of Fame broadcaster Tom Jackson dies at 75

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Denver Broncos legend and Ring of Fame linebacker Tom Jackson has died at the age of 75, the team announced on Sunday. No cause of death was reported.

The Denver Broncos are heartbroken by the passing of Ring of Fame linebacker Tom Jackson, a cornerstone of the legendary Orange Crush defense and one of the most beloved players in franchise history. pic.twitter.com/MdcpkuyKYs

Jackson was a major piece of the "Orange Crush" defense, playing for the team from 1973 to 1986. He was selected by the Broncos in the fourth round of the 1973 NFL Draft and spent his entire career in Denver.

The three-time Pro Bowler played in 191 regular season games, with 177 starts, along with 10 playoff games. He also has two Super Bowl appearances, starting in the 1977 championship game, the Broncos first trip to the big game, and 1986.

His 20 interceptions are tied for the most by a linebacker in team history and his seven picks in the 1976 season are the most by a linebacker in a single season in Broncos history. The first-team All-Pro has a career 40.0 sacks and three touchdowns.

Jackson's excellence throughout his 14 years on the team made him a fan favorite and put him in the Broncos Ring of Fame in 1992.

After he retired, he stayed in the football world as a broadcaster. He was given the Pete Rozelle Award for broadcasting by the Pro Football Hall of Fame in 2015.

Funny ha-ha or funny peculiar? Yesterday had plenty of both

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Here’s a question: did last night’s Newcastle United 2 Arsenal 2 match at St James’ Park show we can be the equal of the reigning Premier League champions?

At 16 minutes past six, after a miserably one-sided first half, you would have been embarking on mission impossible to find anyone saying “yes” among the 52,000+plus in the stadium or the millions watching on TV.

By the final whistle, blown nearly 10 minutes after the 90 by Chris Kavanagh, few would have replied “no”. Such is football.

Let’s start at the beginning, or at least on the 12.30 Azuma train from King’s Cross to Newcastle. The LNER service was predictably packed with Gooners. Passengers in Carriage J were overwhelming confident of a one-nil or two-nil to the Arsenal, as they like to crow in north London.

Based on their team’s results this season, that was to be expected. They don’t score many and, apart from a blip near Brighton, they concede even fewer.

Arsenal are, in a word, efficient. I find them boring. The descriptions are not mutually exclusive. No way as the train rumbled into the magnificent Central Station about 32 minutes late was I looking forward to a feast of entertaining football.

In dire need of a drink, I walked up Pink Lane with Big Nige, who had boarded the delayed train at York after it made an unscheduled stop south of the old Viking stronghold of Jorvik. Known as Eboracum in Roman times, btw.

Pink Lane was awash with happy Londoners. There are many, many reasons to be proud of Newcastle; one is the warm welcome the natives offer strangers (excluding Mackems, obviously. There have to be limits).

Which was why the mild hostility that greeted me in the Mosaic Tap was a big surprise. A young chap called Rob walked up to our table, where Paul and Gowa had already got in the drinks, and said: “You can’t wear that scarf in here.”

His mate had apparently clocked me as a Gooner, having mistaken my half-and-half for an Arsenal favour.

A little bit of background. Said scarf was a souvenir from the Bayer Leverkusen v NUFC match. Not that I bought it on my visit to the BayArena shop a mere 11 months ago, when it would have cost £20. This one was acquired in October for a tenner in the NUFC pre-Christmas online sale, while present-buying for my daughter Jo and her family. The PIF need all the help they can get . . .

For all those who detest a half-and-half, that’s your prerogative. I view it as a reminder of a great trip, more useful than a match programme and less likely to be lost.

Rob quickly realised his error, probably because I had slipped into my best am dram persona by screaming: “Look at the scarf! Look at it! You need to go to Specsavers, mate!”

The faux outrage reduced poor Rob to a gibbering, hilariously apologetic wreck. “Sorry, sorry, I’m really sorry. It was my mate . . . What are you drinking, can I get you one, please?”

The free glass of malbec was much appreciated, thanks. Considering the prices at Mosaic, the scarf has probably paid for itself. Funny ha-ha, Rob.

We played the “who would you select” game and all four of us agreed Gonzalez was not justifying his place. Starting Steur was a no-brainer, the back five picked themselves, based on the Fulham game, with Livramento available if needed to give us a second-half lift. The midfield and attack, apart from Steur and Barnes, were as clear as a peasouper.

The big news at 4.15 was Bruno G starting for his new team. Who had expected that?

There was just time for a quick one in Bar Loco, where I bumped into the legendary Tony Mallabar, before safely negotiating Turnstile 31 at the Leazes End. My seat in row MM was in line with the edge of the six-yard box, which gave a perfect view of the fourth goal, unfortunately.

The Booing of Bruno had been a hot topic. My opinion is that every opposition player should be relentlessly jeered, booed, abused and barracked (isn’t SJP on Barrack Road for a reason?) That’s what I recall, perhaps falsely, from big games in the Seventies . . .

Anyway, the Brazilian midfielder was booed from the warm-up until his replacement by Lewis-Skelly when we were leading 2-1. The effort of singling out our former captain probably backfired, because it distracted the more vocal supporters from doing the job of cheering their favourites.

Not that the players or the play gave reasons to be buoyant in the first half. Arsenal did what Arsenal do, using their 11 players against our outfield 10. Whenever Osula or Willock or Murphy or Barnes or Steur attempted to put pressure on Raya, he found the spare man whom our would-be presser had just left unmarked. Boring and efficient by Arsenal. Half-cocked and aimless by the Mags.

In my opinion, Arsenal are no great shakes, whatever the title says. A great team have a top, top striker. They don’t. As they proved by managing only one effort on target before the interval. Hornicek didn’t have a prayer once Hall chested down a long ball and instantly lost possession near the edge of his box.

Our left-back is a wonderful footballer but, having rarely played as a defender in his youth at Chelsea, he is still learning some of the skills essential to be a watertight obstacle.

Murphy tried to catch Raya off his line with a speculative long-range effort and one other shot flew narrowly wide of Raya’s left post via a deflection off Barnes. There was almost nothing else of note in the first half as the champions biffed nearly every shot high, wide and ugly.

Burn and Thiaw stood reasonably firm, Dedic and Murphy seemed on different wavelengths, Steur harried Bruno G and Barnes showed some unlikely aggression. Miley plugged away and Osula ran gamely but United lacked any sort of perceptible gameplan. We rarely managed to find a player in space. Worrying times.

Where was our next goal coming from, wondered my mate Dave at the interval. It had been a while at SJP.

The answer came within four minutes of the restart, soon after the first of three stoppages caused by apparent injuries to Raya. More importantly, after Matthias Jaissle made a triple substitution.

Harvey Barnes smashed a shot low to the keeper’s right, ending a quick attack. Willock had pressured Odegaard into presenting the ball to Osula. An assist on the headless chicken’s credits, fitting reward for another tireless display.

This was when the match became funny peculiar. Miley man-marked Bruno, Arsenal misplaced passes, United won 50:50s, we found space to run with purpose. The gulf in monetary and player resources vanished as the Mags reverted to our best version of Howe-ball.

Only Hornicek of the summer 2026 signings was still on the pitch, Steur having been subbed.

Arsenal looked distinctly second-best. Raya went down again. A corner on our right from Hall was aimed beyond the far post, where the unstoppable Man from Blyth powered a header goalwards. In a frantic kerfuffle, a couple of blocks merely delayed the inevitable. Thiaw smashed it home and we were ahead.

Around me in row MM, the earlier, almost incessant moans and grounds became voices of encouragement. Supporters who had failed to back their team in a dire first half were suddenly full of the joys of November . . .

Ramsey, Livramento and Elanga all caused Arsenal problems. For a few minutes, we battered them.

Then Raya collapsed for a third time. The long delay gave the champions a break they desperately needed.

Whatever anyone else says, Jaissle and his men were right to keep pressing for a third. Arsenal were always going to fashion at least one good chance in the time remaining after we went ahead. To sit back with 28 minutes left, plus a lot of stoppage time, would have been professional suicide.

My suspicion is that those who now bemoan our aggressive play would have whinged even more if the team had sat back. The match became attack and counter-attack. Arsenal, through Madueke doing a decent impression of the absent Saka, kept cutting onto his left foot. Hall, sometimes having to mark two opponents, let the right winger swerve inside and slam a low shot through two defenders and past Hornicek.

The keeper got two hands to the ball but could only push it into the Leazes End net.

Havertz nearly produced what would have been a truly wonderful moment when his head diverted a late Hall corner beyond Kepa and against the crossbar. So near, yet so far.

Then Barnes capped what I reckon was his best game since Wembley 2025 by outstripping Rice on the left touchline and playing the ball perfectly to Ramsey on the edge of the 18-yard box.

In the words of John Ando Anderson, just hit the bloody thing, man. Ramsey did. Sadly, though, instead of putting his laces through it, he tried to bend the ball into the postage stamp and it flew high and wide.

What a feather in Jaissle’s cap a win would have been. One point was good, three would have been great.

The late introduction of Wissa, who replaced Willock, was a failure. He contributed almost nothing, which has been the story all too often since his expensive arrival.

I would have thrown on Fernandez-Pardo as the game opened up. No team enjoy speedy opponents running hard at them and the Belgium international could have been the joker in Jaissle’s pack.

On the balance of play a draw was reasonable but this, like the Liverpool result, felt like two points dropped.

What is inarguable is the team’s willingness to battle for their mates, their manager and the Toon. Long may that continue.

How Diego Forlan fought to become a Manchester United cult hero

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Even those who watched Diego Forlan closest disagree over whether he was any good at Manchester United.

Sir Alex Ferguson remembers the striker as a “tough, grand player”. But Gary Neville, in his autobiography, lumped Forlan in with Kleberson, David Bellion and Liam Miller as the epitome of the ‘Djemba-Djemba years’.

Regardless of whether or not he was rated, Forlan remains revered by those on the Stretford End all these years later.

Forlan’s song is still sung with gusto around Old Trafford and by travelling Reds, especially around meetings with Liverpool. It was at Anfield where the forward scored the two goals that cemented his place in United fans’ hearts and their place in his.

“I heard the 3,000 fans in the away end loud and clear,” he wrote in his column for The National in 2016. “‘Diego’, they sang, ‘Wooahh, Diego, wooaah. He came from Uruguay, he made the Scousers cry.’

“I allowed myself a second to enjoy the moment. These fans sang with their Manchester accents louder than anything the Liverpool fans could come up with.

“As they sang their beautiful song that they still sing to this day, I thought: ‘I am a lucky man. This is the type of moment that you dream of as a boy, the type of thing I’ve worked so hard for.'”

Forlan’s Anfield heroics came in the middle of a three-month period that was by far his most productive spell in a United shirt – a time that ensured he will always be remembered fondly at Old Trafford.

Those goals against Liverpool and splendid late winners against Southampton and Chelsea were just-reward for Forlan’s graft. Like so many cult heroes, what the forward lacked in ability and luck, he compensated for with spirit, determination and hard graft.

That’s enough for some people. “Ole Gunnar Solskjaer, my former Manchester United team-mate, once told me that I train harder than any player he’s played with,” Forlan has since said.

Even football’s harshest critic, Roy Keane, was willing to cut Forlan some slack on the basis of his application and hunger.

“If a player tried – and Diego did – we’d drag him with us; we’d try and help him,” he said. “Diego was honest, so in training you’d go, ‘Unlucky; it’ll come good tomorrow,’ not, ‘You can f***** do better than that.'”

Forlan may well be the only player to make Keane feel empathy. But the skipper could see there was a player in Forlan, and though he was rarely able to show it at United, it wasn’t for the lack of trying.

Of course, that talent did eventually shine through – almost blindingly so. And almost as soon as he was sold by United at close to a £5million loss.

In the seven seasons that followed his United stint, Forlan took Spain by storm, first with Villarreal, then during four seasons at Atletico Madrid.

The Uruguayan won the Pichichi Trophy and the European Golden Shoe twice each before peaking at age 31, when he scored twice in the Europa League final before winning the Golden Ball for the best player at the 2010 World Cup.

So why did it not work out for Forlan at Old Trafford?

The stick most often taken to Forlan’s United spell is his goalscoring record – 17 in 98 appearances.

It’s hardly unfair to judge a forward on his goals return, but in Forlan’s case, context is required. Only a little over a third of those appearances came from the start.

Furthermore, of his 61 appearances as a substitute, 43 of them involved less than 20 minutes’ work. He had less than 10 minutes to impress on 16 of those occasions.

Some players simply do not thrive coming off the bench, and Forlan was one of them. Only five of his goals for United were scored as a sub.

Conversely, played from the off, the striker’s ratio was a goal every three starts. Hardly prolific, but certainly more creditable.

Unfortunately for Forlan, with Ole Gunnar Solskjaer halfway through his sixth and most prolific season for United, and Ruud van Nistelrooy already on 24 goals by the time he joined, starting opportunities were hard to come by.

And when he was handed the chance to play alongside Van Nistelrooy, it didn’t work.

In Ferguson’s words, Forlan “ran up against the problem of Ruud’s singularity”.

“Ruud wanted to be the number one finisher, that was his nature. Diego Forlan didn’t register on his radar at all, so when you put the two of them out there, there was zero chemistry.

“Diego was better with a partner. But he scored some priceless goals. He was a good player and a terrific pro.”

Forlan’s first half-season at the club saw him make seven starts and 11 substitute appearances, but the striker finished the campaign goalless, with United trophyless.

The United fans remained supportive but more in sympathy rather than expectation as 26 goalless appearances passed – 19 of those off the bench – before the misfiring striker’s moment arrived.

United were already 4-1 up in their Champions League group opener at home to Maccabi Haifa when Forlan was dispatched from the bench. With the contest effectively over, Forlan had 35 minutes against the Israelis to scavenge for his first goal.

Thirty-four of them had passed before United were awarded a penalty and David Beckham presented the ball to his desperate team-mate, who placed it on the spot facing the Stretford End.

“Please. please. Anyone with an ounce of humanity says please on his behalf,” begged ITV commentator Peter Drury as Old Trafford watched an otherwise inconsequential penalty through its fingers.

Forlan took a five-step run-up and rammed the ball into the bottom corner, sending the goalkeeper the wrong way in a manner that belied whatever nervousness he must surely have been feeling.

Forlan’s own celebration was far more muted than those of his team-mates.

That could not be said, however, when he broke his Premier League duck one start and six substitute appearances later, with United searching for an equaliser at home to Aston Villa.

After directing home a powerful header from Mikaël Silvestre’s floated pass, Forlan’s shirt was off, with the equaliser enjoyed with far more gusto than the usual ‘grab the ball and get back to the halfway line’ routine usually seen from Ferguson’s sides.

Forlan’s chest was bare three times in a little under three months during the period that saw him write his name into United folklore.

He endeared himself to the Old Trafford faithful further still a week after his first Premier League goal, when he came off the bench to smash in a brilliant, dipping 25-yard winner with five minutes remaining against Southampton.

Just as memorable as that strike to those present on that November day was the sight of a half-naked Forlan tracking back to make a tackle with his shirt still in his hand after the Saints had restarted.

United’s new Nike jerseys that season had integrated base layers, which the players hated and took scissors to. But not Forlan, apparently, who more than once struggled to solve the puzzle of turning the garment the right way around in a timely fashion having just whipped it off in a frenzy.

The white shirt remained on his back, however, at Anfield on the day that United fans remember so gleefully – and that Jerzy Dudek still has nightmares about.

The Liverpool keeper dropped the kind of bollock you simply never expect to see from a professional goalkeeper, let alone an international.

Dudek bent a knee to scoop up Jamie Carragher’s back header, but his elbows and legs opened like the Mersey Tunnel as the ball passed through him as if he was a hologram.

Forlan accepted his gift, but the forward still had to roll the ball into the empty goal only four yards away. The angle was hardly acute, but the traveling Reds still held their breath while their No.22 took a touch, took aim and very precisely sidefoted into the far side netting.

Forlan’s surge in confidence was evident almost immediately, when Dudek was put on his back for the second time in three minutes. This time, after gathering Ryan Giggs’ toe-poked pass, the forward smashed the ball inside the Liverpool keeper’s near post.

Forland described in his column for The National in 2015 how that moment felt: “This time, I ran to the away end and the fans went crazy.

“Faces looked like they going to explode. My team-mates were delighted, for the team and for me as they knew I’d had a tough time. Stewards rushed forward to push us away from the fans.

“Ryan Giggs, who set me up for the goal, told the stewards in bright orange jackets where they should go.

“I repeated what Giggs said. Nobody was stopping us enjoying this moment. Nobody.

“I wanted to jump over that fence and get in with the fans, which is not responsible, but I was so happy and my adrenaline was pumping.”

Forlan, who has stated his desire to watch the Red Devils play at Liverpool from that Anfield Road end, had perhaps only more more major adrenaline rush in a United shirt.

That came after he lashed Juan Sebastian Veron’s exquisite split pass into the roof of Carlo Cudicini’s net in added time to give the Red Devils a precious 2-1 win over Chelsea. Cue more chest baring and shirt faffing…

Seven goals in his first full campaign at Old Trafford helped United reclaim the Premier League title back from Arsenal, but despite matching that haul the following season, it was clear Forlan was fading further out of the picture as Ferguson put his faith in a lone-striker system.

A goalless run of nine appearances was broken with four goals in four games in a 10-day period taking in three different competitions, but only two more Premier League goals followed – an injury-time brace in a 4-0 win over Aston Villa – before Ferguson signed Louis Saha from Fulham.

Forlan played only seven more times in the Premier League, finishing on the losing side on three occasions and failing to score a goal.

United finished third and saved their season only by winning the FA Cup. Forlan was not included in the squad for the final.

Forlan was flogged to Spain that summer, but he certainly doesn’t deserve to be lumped in the Djemba-Djemba group. United have had some much less successful strikers both before and since.

Forlan doesn’t look upon his time at Old Trafford as a failure, though nor would he claim it went as well as he might have realistically hoped.

But it should not be forgotten that Forlan was still a developing talent when he landed in Britain. Only 22 years old with fewer than 100 senior games behind him, he was a late bloomer, as his subsequent achievements demonstrate.

He eventually peaked in his thirties – at least a couple of years later than many professional footballers.

In 2008-09, Forlan won the Pichichi Trophy after scoring 32 goals in 33 La Liga matches, as well as the European Golden Boot.

After his 31st birthday, the Uruguay hitman won the Golden Ball, the Golden Boot and Goal of the Tournament at the 2010 World Cup.

Forlan’s personal career highlight did not come until he had turned 32, when he helped Uruguay become Copa America champions, meaning his family had become the first to have three winners of the competition from three generations.

That was 10 years after United brought Forlan to Europe. Still developing back then, the truth is, he neither over-achieved nor under-performed at United.

But supporters remember moments more readily than numbers which is why they still serenade the man who came from Uruguay and made the Scousers cry.

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

On March 5, 2023, Liverpool recorded an emphatic 7-0 win over Manchester United – but how well do you remember the game?

Jurgen Klopp’s side took the lead on the stroke of half time and they then became absolutely relentless in the second half by smashing in a further six goals.

The result is Liverpool’s biggest-ever triumph over the Red Devils and it’s also one of the biggest wins in Premier League history.

Following the dominant performance, Klopp told reporters: “Freak result, top performance. A really top performance from the start, I thought the way we started the game was really special, the best for a long, long, long, long, long, long time. We were there, we were aggressive but we played football.

“It was one of the best performances for a long, long time. In a way I think everybody saw how good the boys can be.”

Former ESPN analyst and NFL linebacker Tom Jackson dies at 75 years old

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Longtime ESPN analyst and former NFL linebacker Tom Jackson has died. He was 75.

Jackson was a fixture on ESPN's NFL coverage after his playing career ended in 1986. He joined the network in 1987 and worked alongside Chris Berman for nearly 30 years until his retirement in 2016.

Berman eulogized his friend and colleague during ESPN's "Sunday NFL Countdown," expressing his shock after seeing Jackson weeks ago at the network's studios in Bristol, Connecticut and talking to him last week.

"All of us are stunned. We've lost one of the best human beings ever to walk the planet," Berman said. "I was fortunate, as you know, to work side-by-side with him for 30 years on 'NFL Primetime' and 'NFL Gameday,' now 'Sunday NFL Countdown.'"

"Tom was so much more than an accomplished NFL analyst," he added. "There was nobody kinder, there was nobody more thoughtful, more loyal, more engaging than Tom Jackson."

Jackson died on Friday, according to Berman. His family informed ESPN of his death soon thereafter.

Before going into broadcasting, Jackson was a linebacker for the Denver Broncos for 14 seasons. The team's 1975 fourth-round pick out of Louisville, he was named to three Pro Bowls and twice earned All-Pro honors. The Broncos inducted Jackson into their Ring of Fame in 1992.

Yet Jackson is known to generations of NFL fans for his work providing commentary on highlights for ESPN with Berman, long before pro football studio shows like "NFL Live" and the NFL Network became ubiquitous.

Watching clips of the day's games was never more electric than when the two detailed the action on screen with ESPN's iconic background music playing behind them during "NFL Primetime."

One of Jackson's signature bits was showing the week's hardest hits on Monday night, saying players got "Jacked Up!" Though that segment was eventually phased out as concern and consideration for head trauma and serious injury grew, Jackson conveyed the excitement and thrill that so many fans felt while watching the NFL.

ELLE Decor Checks In: The Cooper, Charleston

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The South Carolina city’s first waterfront hotel features nautical-inspired interiors by Meyer Davis and Champalimaud Design.

Founded as a seaport more than 350 years ago, Charleston evolved into one of the most important trade hubs in colonial America. As the South Carolina city’s wealth grew, handsome estates began filling up its coastline. But throughout Charleston’s rich, colorful, and complicated history, the city never had a high-end hotel that took full advantage of its waterfront setting—until this year, with the debut of The Cooper.

Located on the site of the former State Ports Authority headquarters (which was demolished in 2019), the 191-key property sits on Charleston’s historic peninsula and overlooks the Cooper River. Interiors by Champalimaud Design pay homage to the city’s maritime heritage, referencing the “Lowcountry” culture that took root in the surrounding region.

“We wanted The Cooper to feel like a natural extension of the city, balancing the ease of a waterfront home with the refinement of a luxury hotel,” says Jun Chun, principal at Champalimaud Design. “The overall inspiration was Charleston itself, particularly its coastal heritage, architectural history, and gracious, unhurried way of life.”

The six-story property, which is operated by Beemok Hospitality Collection, has a relaxed feel infused with a subtle Southern flavor. The experience starts before guests even step inside thanks to an entryway lined with an allée of crepe myrtle trees—a Charleston staple with tumbles of showy, hot-pink blooms.

Inside, the lobby sports creamy marble flooring with a basketweave pattern that nods to the local art of braiding sweetgrass. Meanwhile, a mural inspired by changing tides lines the spiral staircase, leading to the public areas upstairs.

The hotel’s Mediterranean-style signature restaurant, The Crossing, helmed by chef Nick Dugan, was designed by multidisciplinary studio Meyer Davis. (There are three additional, smaller dining venues on site.) For the restaurant’s meandering floor plan, the design team created a series of distinct indoor and outdoor spaces, hewing to a marshland palette of soothing neutrals and calming blues as well as strategic doses of lacquer, teak, and honey onyx.

References to the local culture continue in the guest rooms, which feature bespoke headboards, wardrobes, and credenzas adorned with woven details, once again tying back to vernacular craft. “We approached the guest rooms as elegant waterfront homes rather than traditional hotel rooms,” says Chun. “Wide-plank oak floors, painted shiplap walls, woven textures, and residential-style Roman shades establish a sense of warmth and familiarity.”

Among the amenities are a 7,000-square-foot spa as well as a heated saltwater infinity-edge pool on the fourth-floor terrace. The pool deck, which has its own bar, overlooks not just the harbor but even more perks: a new waterfront park by Rees Roberts + Partners plus a private marina, where guests can charter one of the property’s cruising vessels, such as a Hinckley motor yacht.

For the design team, bringing The Cooper to life was a careful balance of respecting tradition while also looking forward. “Charleston has an extraordinary architectural heritage and a strong tradition of craftsmanship, and we wanted to acknowledge those qualities without simply reproducing the past,” explains Chun.

“Ultimately, we wanted The Cooper to feel as though it belongs in Charleston today and will continue to feel relevant for years to come.”

Geoffrey Montes is an associate editor at ELLE Decor with a serious love for all things real estate and design. Before that, he worked at Architectural Digest, Galerie, and Preservation magazines, covering everything from jaw-dropping listings to world-famous architects and design events like Salone del Mobile and Homo Faber.

Lewis Hall transfer could break record as Newcastle set new valuation - Club adviser

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Newcastle United now value left-back Lewis Hall at as much as £80million after his impressive recent performances for England.

That is according to former Everton chief executive Keith Wyness, speaking exclusively to Football Insider, who believes that the 22-year-old is at the "perfect age" to attract major interest in the coming years.

Hall starred in England's recent win over Spain in the Nations League, as he was largely able to keep Lamine Yamal quiet across the 90 minutes.

Wyness believes that the starlet's valuation will have skyrocketed after that display, as he proved once again that he can handle a duel with one of the world's best talents.

Hall previously lined up against Yamal in the Champions League clash with Barcelona at St James' Park back in March, when the Spanish giants needed a last-minute penalty to snatch a draw.

The defender's stock has risen dramatically since that night, and Wyness believes that Newcastle could land an eye-watering sum when he ultimately moves on.

Everton’s former chief Wyness – who served as CEO at Goodison Park between 2004 and 2009 and now runs a football consultancy advising elite clubs – believes that Hall could become the world's most expensive left-back whenever he ultimately leaves Newcastle.

The current record for that position stands at Bayern Munich's £68m move for Lucas Hernandez back in 2019, with Josko Gvardiol still primarily a centre-back when he moved to Manchester City for £77m.

Speaking on the latest edition of Football Insider's Inside Track podcast, Wyness explained how Hall's fee could surpass both of those stars.

"Well, the record for somebody in that position is just over £60m," he said.

"So £100m could be a stretch. There's no doubt he is a talent. And the Newcastle fans will tell you he's been outstanding for a long time now, and he's really got all the signs of a real talent.

"I think you could get as high as £80m is where I think it could go to. And that's purely because of the position that he's in.

"But then again, every position is becoming more and more sought after. He's at the perfect age as well."

Football Insider revealed earlier this week that Newcastle are currently locked in an open race with Sunderland to sign Sheffield United midfielder Sydie Peck.

The 22-year-old is out of contract in 2028, and the pair of North East giants are already paying close attention to his progress.

However, sources say that neither side are close to a deal at the moment, with Sheffield United adamant that Peck won't be sold mid-season.

Newcastle view the England U21 international as a player with a huge future ahead of him, and someone with a big ceiling at the top level.

Sources: Premier League club to swoop for Omari Hutchinson after Nottingham Forest latest

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Senior Correspondent AUTHORITY Senior football correspondent and podcast host with specialist knowledge across the Premier League and SPFL. FOCUS Transfer news, breaking stories, and behind-the-scenes insight across Arsenal, Liverpool, Celtic, and Manchester United. THE INSIGHT Callum utilises a network of club and industry contacts across the Premier League and SPFL to deliver verified, high-speed reporting. He provides the depth behind the headlines — from breaking transfer news to behind-the-scenes developments — ensuring fans get the full story as it happens.

Nottingham Forest could risk losing Omari Hutchinson to one of their Premier League rivals, sources have told Football Insider.

The 23-year-old has been deemed surplus to requirements at the City Ground, and was allowed to join AC Milan on a season-long loan deal during the summer.

After a mixed start to life in Serie A, doubts have emerged about whether Milan will make his deal permanent, placing a number of his potential suitors on high alert.

As I revealed on the latest edition of Football Insider's Transfer Insider podcast, Hutchinson remains highly rated after his spells in the youth setups at Arsenal and Chelsea.

His previous performances in the Premier League for Ipswich Town also alerted top-flight clubs to his talents, and ultimately earned him a £37.5million move to Forest.

While things have not worked out for Hutchinson at Nottingham Forest, clubs are keeping a close eye on his progress at AC Milan, and could swoop to land him next summer.

Football Insider revealed back in September that Hutchinson has no way back at Nottingham Forest, with Oliver Glasner having decided he will not be part of his plans.

After agreeing a loan move to AC Milan during the summer, the Forest boss made the call to cut ties with the attacking midfielder in favour of other stars in his position.

As a result, clubs are keeping a close eye on his performances, and if he does not make the permanent switch to Milan, he could find himself at a new Premier League club.

Speaking on the Transfer Insider podcast, I explained: "That will definitely be an interesting one to watch with Omari Hutchinson. He's been quite highly rated for a long time.

"I remember when he was at Arsenal and being tipped for big things there, obviously then moved to Chelsea and it didn't quite work out.

"He did well during his time at Ipswich, I think that proved he can still be a success in the Premier League despite Forest deciding he would not be part of their plans.

"But I'm sure that if he was to become available, if he didn't join Milan, there would probably be English clubs willing to take that gamble on him.

"They'll be keeping an eye on how he performs over in Italy and if he is to leave Forest permanently, because as I said, he does have a number of admirers and has for a while."

Much of whether Hutchinson is allowed to join another Premier League club will depend on AC Milan, as they weigh up triggering the option to buy in his loan deal.

Last month, Hutchinson starred in AC Milan's 4-0 win over RB Salzburg in the Europa League, and has since established himself as a valuable asset in Ruben Amorim's side.

As a result, Football Insider reported that Hutchinson may be edging closer to a premanent move to the San Siro, and they could trigger his £34million buy option.

With AC Milan also paying a small loan fee, the Reds might actually be able to make a small profit on Hutchinson despite having paid £37.5million to land him in 2025.

There are doubts about whether the Italian giants will be able to afford such a significant fee, however, will all eyes on how Hutchinson performs this season.
